+//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
+use super::Parser;
+use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
+impl Parser {
+    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
+        self.take("(")?;
+        let mut args = Vec::new();
+        while !self.at(")") {
+            let name = self.peek().clone();
+            let binding = self.local_name()?;
+            self.take(":")?;
+            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
+            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
+                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
+                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
+                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
+                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
+                }
+                d
+            })?;
+            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
+                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
+                self.position += 1;
+                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
+                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
+                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
+                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
+            }
+            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
+            if !self.maybe(",") {
+                break;
+            }
+        }
+        self.take(")")?;
+        Ok(args)
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let mut kind;
+        if self.maybe("[") {
+            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
+            self.take("]")?;
+            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
+            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
+            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
+                if parser.at("-") {
+                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
+                }
+                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
+                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
+                if token.value.contains('.') {
+                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
+                }
+                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
+            };
+            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                (0, number(self)?)
+            } else {
+                let min = number(self)?;
+                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
+                (min, number(self)?)
+            };
+            if min > max {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
+            }
+            if item.optional {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
+            }
+            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
+        } else {
+            let name = self.word()?;
+            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
+                "text" => {
+                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                        (None, self.number()?)
+                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
+                        let min = self.number()?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
+                    } else {
+                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
+                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
+                        (None, 10_000)
+                    };
+                    let mut format = None;
+                    if self.maybe("format") {
+                        let value = self.word()?;
+                        if value.value != "handle" && value.value != "url" {
+                            let bound = match min {
+                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
+                                None => format!("<={max}"),
+                            };
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle or text {bound} format url")));
+                        }
+                        format = Some(value.value);
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
+                }
+                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
+                "money" => {
+                    let currency = self.word()?;
+                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
+                    }
+                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
+                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
+                }
+                "json" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "bytes" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "image" => {
+                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
+                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
+                }
+                "video" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
+                }
+                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
+                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
+                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    let max = self.number()?;
+                    let mut types = Vec::new();
+                    if self.maybe("(") {
+                        while !self.at(")") {
+                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
+                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
+                            }
+                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
+                            }
+                            types.push(value.value);
+                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
+                        }
+                        self.take(")")?;
+                        if types.is_empty() {
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
+                        }
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
+                }
+                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
+                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
+                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
+                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
+                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;
+                    TypeKind::IntRange { min, max }
+                }
+                "int" => TypeKind::Int,
+                "time" => TypeKind::Time,
+                "bool" => TypeKind::Bool,
+                "enum" => {
+                    self.take("(")?;
+                    let mut variants = Vec::new();
+                    while !self.at(")") {
+                        variants.push(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value);
+                        if !self.maybe(",") {
+                            break;
+                        }
+                    }
+                    self.take(")")?;
+                    if variants.is_empty() {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&site, "enum needs at least one variant", "enum('draft', 'published')"));
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Enum { variants }
+                }
+                other => TypeKind::Ref { table: other.to_owned() },
+            };
+        }
+        if self.maybe("once") {
+            if !column {
+                let optional = self.maybe("?");
+                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is a column modifier, not an argument constraint; declare it on the owning table column", print::scalar_type(&ScalarType { kind, optional })).mechanical());
+            }
+            match &mut kind {
+                TypeKind::Image { once, .. } => *once = true,
+                TypeKind::Video { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; video ranges are unspecified", "use photo: image <=10000000 once, or remove once from the video column")),
+                TypeKind::File { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; a downloaded file is saved by the browser, so one view cannot be kept", "remove once from the file column; use image <=10000000 once for one-view photos").guide("assets")),
+                _ => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns", "remove once from this column; use image <=10000000 once for one-use images")),
+            }
+        }
+        let optional = self.maybe("?");
+        Ok(ScalarType { kind, optional })
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn media_size(&mut self) -> Fallible<u32> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let size = self.number()?;
+        let multiplier = match self.peek().value.as_str() { "KB" => 1_000, "MB" => 1_000_000, "GB" => 1_000_000_000, _ => return Ok(size) };
+        self.position += 1;
+        size.checked_mul(multiplier).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&site, "upload bound exceeds 4,294,967,295 bytes", "use image <=5MB or a byte count fitting u32").guide("types"))
+    }
+}
//! Developer-owned, in-memory refusal context. Never part of Refusal or receipts.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — only a host opt-in can collect the private refusing clause.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{RuntimeError, schema::Effect};

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct RefusingClause {
    pub table: String,
    pub viewer: String,
    pub clause: String,
    pub phase: String,
    pub terminal: bool,
    pub no_candidate: bool,
    pub site: String,
    pub generation: u64,
}
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct RefusalTrace(std::sync::Arc<std::sync::Mutex<Option<RefusingClause>>>, std::sync::Arc<std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64>);
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl RefusalTrace {
    /// Physical row images encountered and filtered by read authority in this request.
    pub fn hidden(&self) -> u64 { self.1.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) }
    pub fn take(&self) -> Option<RefusingClause> { self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).take() }
}
impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.031#1.2 — only a real, uncapped storage miss; never replace a clause.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(super) fn no_candidate(&self, table: &str, site: &str) {
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get_or_insert_with(|| RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: "no-candidate".into(), phase: "storage probe".into(),
                terminal: false, no_candidate: true, site: site.into(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.refusing_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.filtered_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, false);
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, true);
    }
    fn record_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str, terminal: bool) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            *trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()) = Some(RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: clause.into(), phase: phase.into(), terminal, no_candidate: false, site: self.statement_site.clone(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, clause, phase, terminal);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_rule_result(&self, result: &Result<bool, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() {
            let phase = format!("{effect} rule");
            if matches!(result, Ok(false)) { self.record_clause(table, &phase, &phase, effect != Effect::Read); }
            self.trace_authorization(result, table, effect, &phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect);
    }
    /// Preserve the phase before enclosing writes remap the public error effect.
    pub(super) fn trace_authorization<T>(&self, result: &Result<T, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect, phase: &str) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() && matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. })) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect, phase);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_unlocated_budget(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.as_ref().is_some_and(|trace| trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e|e.into_inner()).as_ref().is_none_or(|cause| !cause.clause.starts_with("authorization allowance"))) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), "authorization access");
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, effect);
    }
    pub(super) fn hidden_row(&self) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace { trace.1.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed); }
    }
    pub(super) fn rule_refusal(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect, clause: &str) -> RuntimeError {
        self.refusing_clause(table, clause);
        RuntimeError::Rule { table: table.into(), effect }
    }
}

use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleExpr {
    Public { reason: String },
    Allow,
    Deny,
    Eq(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    Ne(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    /// Ordered comparisons are confined to named update transitions and to
    /// insert rules over the row's own required int/time columns.
    Compare(crate::ir::BinaryOp, RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    And(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Or(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Not(Box<RuleExpr>),
    Exists {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
    /// True only when the indexed target row was inserted earlier in this
    /// same transaction; insert rules only.
    Created {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn is_public(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. })
    }

    pub fn is_uniformly_readable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow)
    }

    /// Every `.field` the rule reads, on any probe key or comparison.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
            if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } = term {
                out.insert(field.clone());
            }
        };
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow | Self::Deny => {}
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    /// The disjuncts of the rule: an `or` flattens, anything else is itself.
    pub fn disjuncts(&self) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
        match self {
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| item.disjuncts()).collect(),
            other => vec![other],
        }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.6 — service seats read on the server and never enlarge a device's audience.
    pub fn service_grant(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)))
            | Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)), RuleTerm::Viewer)
                if principal.strip_prefix("service:").is_some_and(|name| !name.is_empty()) => Some(principal),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove only entire service disjuncts; a conjunct, negation or ordinary
    /// principal equality must still pass the ordinary exact-audience check.
    pub fn device_audience_rule(&self) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, RuleExpr> {
        let terms = self.disjuncts();
        if !terms.iter().any(|term| term.service_grant().is_some()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(self); }
        let mut audience: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().filter(|term| term.service_grant().is_none()).cloned().collect();
        std::borrow::Cow::Owned(match audience.len() {
            0 => Self::Deny,
            1 => audience.pop().expect("one audience"),
            _ => Self::Or(audience),
        })
    }

//! Authorization shapes that compile and run, but grant or withhold more than
//! the author meant (app-farm wave 6 #22, lanes 001 061 062 073). Each is a
//! review note decided from the schema and the programs' own text; none
//! changes a rule.
//!
//! - `N_PROBE_ONLY_READ`: a read-rule disjunct probes a private table through
//!   the row's reference but names no caller (`exists applications[.application]`).
//!   A rule probe sees every row, so it admits every caller whenever the
//!   parent exists, including callers who cannot read the parent.
//! - `N_VACUOUS_RULE`: an insert rule that every inserting mutation satisfies
//!   for every caller, by writing `viewer` into the column it tests, on a row
//!   that names another principal the caller chose as a reader.
//! - `N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN`: an update or delete rule admits callers the read rule
//!   does not, and a mutation looks the row up by key, so those callers get
//!   `NOT_FOUND` before the write rule runs.
//! - `N_WRITE_DENIED` (counter): a mutation increments a column whose table's
//!   update rule admits only role holders, without requiring the role, so
//!   every other caller is refused `E_RULE` at the counter.
//! - `N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN` (wave 7, lane 001): `require exists t[col = x]` where
//!   every disjunct of t's read rule admits only the row whose `col` is the
//!   caller: a program probe reads under the read rule, so it passes only
//!   when `x` is the caller.
//! - `N_REQUIRE_REFUSED` (wave 7, lane 072): a disjunct of a `require` admits
//!   callers (`person = viewer`, the deleted row's member) whom no disjunct of
//!   the table's delete rule admits: they pass the require and are refused
//!   `E_RULE` at the delete.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.4 — the four lints: precise conditions, so the guide and the farm corpus stay silent.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{Site, ast::*, diag::Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    probe_only_reads(catalog, schema, &mut notes);
    let mutations: Vec<&ProgramDecl> = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.programs)
        .filter(|program| program.kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && !program.native).collect();
    vacuous_inserts(catalog, schema, &mutations, &mut notes);
    hidden_lookups(catalog, schema, &mutations, &mut notes);
    denied_counters(catalog, schema, &mutations, &mut notes);
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#17.5 — wave 7 lanes 001 072: a `require` the table's own rules decide against.
    let programs: Vec<&ProgramDecl> = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.programs)
        .filter(|program| matches!(program.kind, ProgramKind::Mutation | ProgramKind::Query) && !program.native).collect();
    hidden_requires(catalog, schema, &programs, &mut notes);
    let guard_programs = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.programs).filter(|p| !p.native).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    unreadable_guards(catalog, schema, &guard_programs, &mut notes);
    refused_requires(catalog, schema, &mutations, &mut notes);
    notes
}

fn rule_site<'a>(catalog: &'a Catalog, table: &str, effect: Effect) -> Option<&'a Site> {
    catalog.tables.get(table)?.decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == effect).map(|rule| &rule.site)
}

fn mentions_viewer(rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    let term = |t: &RuleTerm| matches!(t, RuleTerm::Viewer);
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a, b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, a, b) => term(a) || term(b),
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(mentions_viewer),
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => mentions_viewer(inner),
        RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, t)| t)).any(term),
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => false,
    }
}

fn conjuncts(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
    match rule { RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().flat_map(conjuncts).collect(), other => vec![other] }
}

/// The probes in `rule`, outside any `not`, of a table whose read rule is not public.
fn private_probes<'a>(schema: &Schema, rule: &'a RuleExpr) -> Vec<&'a RuleExpr> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, key, .. } if key.iter().any(|t| matches!(t, RuleTerm::Field(_)))
            && schema.table(table).is_some_and(|target| !target.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() && target.through.is_none()) => vec![rule],
        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| private_probes(schema, item)).collect(),
        _ => Vec::new(),
    }
}

// ---- N_PROBE_ONLY_READ (lane 073) ----

fn probe_only_reads(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(name) else { continue };
        if info.maintained.is_some() || info.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || table.through.is_some() { continue; }
        let Some(site) = rule_site(catalog, name, Effect::Read) else { continue };
        for disjunct in table.rules.read.disjuncts() {
            if mentions_viewer(disjunct) { continue; }
            // A probe whose own tests make the parent readable to everyone
            // (`exists decks[.deck, isPublic = true]` under `read <- … or .isPublic = true`)
            // discloses nothing the parent does not.
            let public_by_probe = |probe: &RuleExpr| {
                let RuleExpr::Exists { table: parent, equalities, .. } = probe else { return false };
                let pins: BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::Value> = equalities.iter().filter_map(|(column, term)| match term { RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some((column.clone(), value.clone())), _ => None }).collect();
                snapback4_core::engine::rules::pinned_probe_readable(schema, probe)
                    || schema.table(parent).is_some_and(|target| decided(&target.rules.read, &pins) == Some(true))
            };
            let Some(RuleExpr::Exists { table: parent, key, .. }) = private_probes(schema, disjunct).into_iter().find(|probe| !public_by_probe(probe)) else { continue };
            let Some(RuleTerm::Field(reference)) = key.iter().find(|t| matches!(t, RuleTerm::Field(_))) else { continue };
            let Some(parent_table) = schema.table(parent) else { continue };
            // The parent's own reader, to name in the probe.
            let owner = parent_table.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().find_map(|d| match d {
                RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.clone()),
                _ => None,
            });
            let named = owner.map(|column| format!("; or name the caller in the probe (`exists {parent}[.{reference}, {column} = viewer]`) to admit only that reader")).unwrap_or_default();
            let through = if table.rules.read.disjuncts().len() == 1 { format!("`read through .{reference}` admits exactly the {parent} row's readers") } else { format!("to admit the {parent} row's readers, name them in this disjunct, or make the whole rule `read through .{reference}`") };
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "N_PROBE_ONLY_READ",
                format!("table {name}: the read rule's `{}` names no caller, so it admits every signed-in caller whenever that {parent} row exists, including callers who cannot read it: a rule probe sees every {parent} row, it does not inherit {parent}'s read rule", disjunct.quarry()),
                format!("{through}{named}; keep it as written only if every caller may read these {name} rows while the {parent} row exists")).guide("rules"));
            break;
        }
    }
}

/// `rule` for every caller, given only the literal values `pins` of some
/// columns: `Some(true)` when it holds whoever reads, `None` when it depends
/// on the caller or an unpinned column.
fn decided(rule: &RuleExpr, pins: &BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::Value>) -> Option<bool> {
    let value = |t: &RuleTerm| match t { RuleTerm::Field(column) => pins.get(column).cloned(), RuleTerm::Literal(v) => Some(v.clone()), _ => None };
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => Some(true),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Some(false),
        RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) => Some(value(a)? == value(b)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(a, b) => Some(value(a)? != value(b)?),
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => decided(inner, pins).map(|v| !v),
        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().map(|item| decided(item, pins)).try_fold(true, |all, v| match v { Some(false) => Err(()), Some(true) => Ok(all), None => Ok(false) }).map_or(Some(false), |all| all.then_some(true)),
        RuleExpr::Or(items) => {
            let values: Vec<Option<bool>> = items.iter().map(|item| decided(item, pins)).collect();
            if values.contains(&Some(true)) { Some(true) } else if values.iter().all(|v| *v == Some(false)) { Some(false) } else { None }
        }
        _ => None,
    }
}

// ---- N_VACUOUS_RULE (lane 061) ----

/// `{ field: value }` of one insert, with `{ name }` read as `name: name`; None with a spread.
fn written(fields: &[ObjectField]) -> Option<BTreeMap<String, Expr>> {
    fields.iter().map(|field| {
        if field.spread { return None; }
        let value = field.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| Expr::Name { name: field.name.clone(), site: field.site.clone() });
        Some((field.name.clone(), value))
    }).collect()
}

fn is_viewer(expr: &Expr) -> bool { matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" || name == "$input:viewer") }

/// Every write (op, table, keys, fields, site) a program makes, in source order.
fn writes(program: &ProgramDecl) -> Vec<(WriteOp, String, Vec<KeyPart>, Vec<ObjectField>, Site)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let mut body = program.prelude.clone();
    body.extend(program.body.iter().cloned());
    super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |expr: &mut Expr| {
        if let Expr::Write { op, table, keys, fields, site, .. } = expr { out.push((op.clone(), table.clone(), keys.clone(), fields.clone(), site.clone())); }
    });
    out
}

/// Every condition the program tests before acting: `require`s and `if`s, at any depth.
fn conditions(body: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<Expr>) {
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => out.push(condition.clone()),
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { out.push(condition.clone()); conditions(then_body, out); conditions(else_body, out); }
            Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => conditions(body, out),
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

fn names_viewer(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
    let mut found = false;
    let mut copy = expr.clone();
    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |e: &mut Expr| { if is_viewer(e) { found = true; } });
                out.push((row, key));
            }
            Ok(out)
        })?;
        for (row, key) in &rows {
            if let Some(row) = row {
                self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes() + row.projection_heap() + row.materialization_heap()
                    + crate::rows::word_heap::key_decode(table.index(index).map_or(1, |i| i.components.len() + 1), key.len()))?;
            }
        }
        Ok(rows)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — row and field authorization precede result pricing.
    pub(super) fn authorized_point(&mut self, read: &Read, index: &Index, key: &[Value], prepaid: bool, trusted: bool, prepaid_examined: bool, absent: &mut bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        let row = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            if !prepaid_examined && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { self.point_cut(read, key); return Ok(None); }
            let scratch = self.meter.mark();
            let row = self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,&read.table,&read.index,key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,&mut runner.meter)
                })?.pop();
            self.meter.release_heap(scratch);
            let Some((mut row,_)) = row else {
                *absent = true;
                self.point_miss(read, key);
                #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                if !self.device && !self.read_capped() { self.no_candidate(&read.table, &read.site); }
                return Ok(None);
            };
            if !self.point_event_held(read, key, &row) {
                return Ok(None);
            }
            if self.device && self.retained_row(&read.table,&row)? { self.components.note(|frame|frame.retained=true); }
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
            row
        } else {
            let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
            // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a hidden, expired or absent candidate's deadlines are discarded with this scope.
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let authorized = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, u64::from(!prepaid_examined))], |runner, scope| {
                #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                let mut encountered = false;
                let Some(row) = runner.live_lookup(scope,&read.table, &read.index, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] Some(&mut encountered))? else {
                    *absent = true;
                    runner.point_miss(read, key);
                    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                    if !encountered && !runner.device && !runner.read_capped() { runner.no_candidate(&read.table, &read.site); }
                    return Ok(None);
                };
                // @ref LLP 3000.016#21a-owned-events-follow-their-source-rows — a held source whose event kind is not fully synced answers nothing.
                if !runner.point_event_held(read, key, &row) {
                    return Ok(None);
                }
                if runner.inactive(&read.table, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
                let provisional = runner.is_provisional(&read.table, &row)?;
                let allowed = if provisional && !runner.context.is_system() {
                    runner.authorize_read(&read.table, &row, prepaid)?
                } else { trusted || runner.check_rule(&read.table, Effect::Read, None, &row)? };
                if !allowed { if !runner.read_capped() {runner.hidden_row();} return Ok(None); }
                // @ref LLP 3000.001#proposed-composition-of-retained-and-authoritative-reads — a delivered left point hit marks only its component, never whole-query coverage or a miss.
                if runner.device && runner.retained_row(&read.table, &row)? { runner.components.note(|frame| frame.retained = true); }
                runner.note_expiry(table, &row);
                let projected = runner.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();

                let visible = projected.into_value(); // drop hidden storage before leaving the internal scope
                Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: runner.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }))
            })?;
            // The live aggregate is an authorized result computation. It must keep
            // its ordinary work budget and cannot publish a partial scalar value.
            let Some(authorized) = authorized else {
                if !*absent && self.read_capped() { self.point_cut(read, key); }
                return Ok(None);
            };
            expiry.emit();
            authorized.into_value()
        };
        // A maintained point read has already reserved its recount scratch (aggregate_point_scratch).
        let row = if prepaid_examined { self.live_aggregate_prepaid(&read.table, row)? } else { self.live_aggregate(&read.table, row)? }.unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }))
    }

    fn point_event_held(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> bool {
        if self.device && !crate::schema::timeline::holds_event(self.schema, &read.table, row) {
            self.event_read_unknown(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
            return false;
        }
        true
    }

    /// A key-authorized synthetic aggregate image (its fields are the caller's
    /// key, the group id derived from it and the disclosed total) is sealed
    /// without a second row lookup: nothing in it came from hidden storage.
    pub(super) fn authorized_synthetic(&self, table: &'r crate::schema::Table, row: Value) -> AuthorizedRow<'r> {
        AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }
    }

//! A request scope plus a response-layer backstop, never a caller channel.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — missing instrumentation must supersede older evidence.
use super::{Recent, Sites};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::{cell::RefCell, sync::{Arc, atomic::Ordering}};

thread_local! { static CURRENT: RefCell<Option<Evidence>> = const { RefCell::new(None) }; }
struct Evidence {
    recent: Recent,
    operation: String,
    request: u64,
    query: bool,
    trace: RefusalTrace,
    sites: Arc<Sites>,
    generation: Option<u64>,
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct RequestScope;
impl Drop for RequestScope { fn drop(&mut self) { CURRENT.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take()); } }
pub(in crate::serve) fn begin(shared: &crate::serve::Shared, path: &str) -> Option<RequestScope> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let recent = shared.refusals.clone();
    let request = recent.requests.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
    CURRENT.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(Evidence { recent,
        operation: path.strip_prefix("/q/").or_else(|| path.strip_prefix("/m/")).unwrap_or(path).into(),
        request, query: path.starts_with("/q/"), trace: Default::default(), sites: Default::default(), generation: None }));
    Some(RequestScope)
}
pub(in crate::serve) fn context(context: Context, sites: Arc<Sites>, generation: u64) -> Context {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let Some(evidence) = c.as_mut() else { return context };
        evidence.sites = sites;
        evidence.generation = Some(generation);
        context.with_refusal_trace(evidence.trace.clone())
    })
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct Pending { recent: Recent, record: Value }
impl Drop for Pending {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.recent.submit(std::mem::take(&mut self.record)); }
}
// Inspect the actual encoded envelope, after overflow substitution. This covers
// every responder using the shared JSON response layer, including receipt replay,
// static admission and future paths with no attached collector. No user data is
// copied into the record. Unknown empty answers enter only the opt-in local
// trace; successful visibility filtering is never a refusal.
pub(in crate::serve) fn response(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Pending> {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let evidence = c.as_mut()?;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
        if evidence.query && evidence.trace.hidden() > 0 {
            eprintln!("dev query: {} hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", evidence.operation, evidence.trace.hidden());
        }
        let code = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why")).and_then(|v| v.get("code")).and_then(Value::as_str);
        let traced = evidence.trace.take();
        let why = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why"));
        // The one filtered read that is evidence about a program refusal: the
        // refusing `t[x] ! else CODE` lookup itself, whose read rule hid the row.
        let hidden = traced.as_ref().filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule").and_then(|cause| {
            let site = why?.get("site")?.as_str()?;
            evidence.sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))).then(|| (cause.table.clone(), evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into())).cloned()))
        });
        let cause = traced.filter(|cause| {
            // A filtered read earlier in a program is not evidence about a
            // later uninstrumented terminal refusal in that same request.
            code.is_none() || cause.terminal
        });
        let empty_answer = code.is_none();
        let code = match code {
            Some(code) => code,
            None if evidence.query && body.get("data").is_some() && body.get("loading") != Some(&Value::Bool(true)) => {
                // Visibility filtering is a successful read, not a refusal.
                if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| !cause.terminal && !cause.no_candidate) { return None; }
                if evidence.recent.trace_empty && empty(&body["data"]) { "EMPTY_READ" }
                else { return None; }
            }
            None => return None,
        };
        let preflight = code == "E_AUTH" && evidence.generation.is_none() && why.and_then(|v| v.get("site")).is_none();
        let mut record = json!({"preflight":preflight,"operation":evidence.operation, "code":code, "requestId":evidence.request,
            "generation":evidence.generation, "clause":"cause not instrumented", "at":snapback4_core::now_ms()});
        // A program's own `require … else CODE` (or `! else CODE`) carries
        // its source site in the refusal itself: the response's own
        // evidence, not an older trace, so the log can name the line.
        if cause.is_none() && let Some(why) = why
            && why.get("rule").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some(code) && let Some(site) = why.get("site").and_then(Value::as_str) {
            record["clause"] = json!("program refusal");
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["siteKind"] = json!("program");
            if let Some((table, rule)) = &hidden {
                record["hiddenBy"] = json!({"table": table, "readRuleSite": rule});
            }
        }
        if empty_answer {
            record["emptyRead"] = json!(true);
            record["cause"] = json!(if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| cause.no_candidate) { "no-candidate" } else { "unrecorded" });
            record["clause"] = json!("no probe miss and no rule cause were recorded");
        }
        if let Some(cause) = cause {
            let clause = evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.phase.clone()))
                .or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.clause.clone())));
            let site = clause.or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "table".into())));
            // Whether the site is the clause's own line or the table's (an omitted rule).
            record["siteKind"] = json!(if clause.is_some() { "clause" } else if site.is_some() { "table" } else { "" });
            record["table"] = json!(cause.table);
            record["clause"] = json!(cause.clause);
            record["phase"] = json!(cause.phase);
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["executionSite"] = json!(cause.site);
            record["generation"] = json!(cause.generation);
            record["readRuleSite"] = json!(evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table, "read rule".into())));
        }
        // Published before sending: why refuses an old disk snapshot even when
        // the writer is blocked, this handoff drops, or responses finish out of order.
        Some(Pending { recent: evidence.recent.clone(), record: evidence.recent.sequence(record) })
    })
}
fn empty(value: &Value) -> bool {
    match value {
        Value::Null => true,
        Value::Array(values) => values.is_empty(),
        _ => false,
    }
}

//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];

        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,

            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };

                )], |runner,_| {

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
