impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — the dependency envelope is schema-derived too; short-circuit probes cannot disclose a hidden answer through tables.
    pub(super) fn rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table).and_then(|m|m.row_key.as_ref()) {
                self.read_tables.insert(key.table.clone());
            }
            self.aggregate_source_dependency(&table);
            self.read_tables.insert(table);
        }
    }

    fn metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .filter(|maintain| self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring()) {
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn metered_rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let site_heap = rule_probe_sites_heap(rule);
        let site_mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(site_heap)?;
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(row_key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table)
                .and_then(|maintain| maintain.row_key.as_ref()) {
                metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &row_key.table)?;
            }
            self.metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&table)?;
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &table)?;
        }
        self.meter.release_row_heap(site_mark, site_heap);
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — trusted delivery avoids physical probes,
    // not the logical prefix verdict or its scratch admission.
    fn reserve_delivered_scan(&mut self, table: &Table, visibility: rules::Visibility, admitted: bool, query: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && (query || self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort) && !self.context.is_system()
            && admitted && visibility.uniform && !visibility.decided_true && !table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() {
            let allowance = [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))];
            if self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort { self.authorized_scan(&allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            else { self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &allowance, |_, _| Ok(()))?; }
            return Ok(true);
        }
        Ok(false)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — source invalidation is schema-derived, including empty and denied groups.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            && self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring() {
            self.read_tables.insert(maintain.source.clone());
        }
    }

    fn prefix_verdict(&mut self, name: &str, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr, uniform: bool) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if let Some(through) = &table.through && let Some(id) = index.columns().iter().zip(prefix).find_map(|(name,v)| (name == &through.reference).then_some(v)) {
            let verdict = self.parent_verdict(name,through,id)?;
            if verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Unknown { self.unknown_fact(name, crate::schema::through::CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], "read through parent"); }
            let admitted = verdict == super::through::ParentVerdict::Readable;
            return Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
            ));
        }
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one prepaid, restoring scope: a partial conjunction denial costs the same static allowance as a uniform denial.
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&verdict, name, Effect::Read, "read rule");
        let verdict = verdict?;
        if !verdict { self.filtered_clause(name, "read rule"); }
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if uniform && admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    /// A page guard, judged once for the caller (`rules::page_guard_decision`):
    /// a row-independent grant, or a disjunct whose row columns the prefix pins,
    /// evaluated with the prefix's values. It charges the read rule's static
    /// probe count on either verdict, and records no filtered clause: a false
    /// guard leaves the per-row walk.
    fn grant_verdict(&mut self, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        })?;
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

//! One logical allowance for work whose shape can depend on unreadable rows.
use super::runner::Runner;
use std::cell::{Cell, RefCell};

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one physical allowance per request, nested work included.
pub(super) const AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT: usize = 8192;
use crate::{
    assets::Asset,
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{AcquiredBoundary, Bounds, Dir, PrefetchRequest, Rows}, schema::{Index, Table}, value::Value,
};

// Only this module mints the capability; it cannot be constructed or cloned
// by callers, and the borrowed token cannot escape the guarded closure.
pub struct Authorization { probes: Cell<u64>, remaining_probes: u64 }

impl Authorization {
    fn internal() -> Self { Self { probes: std::cell::Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: u64::MAX } }
    // The candidate's declared probe allowance is checked before authorization
    // executes. The enclosing scope commits this logical count on every exit.
    pub(super) fn admit_probes(&self, probes: u64) -> bool {
        let total = self.probes.get().saturating_add(probes);
        self.probes.set(total);
        total <= self.remaining_probes
    }

}


// No Rows implementation, Deref or raw accessor: row reads require the
// unforgeable token even when a caller has access to Runner's store facade.
pub(crate) struct GuardedRows<'a> { raw: &'a dyn Rows, remaining: Cell<usize>, authorizing: Cell<bool>, reference_prepaid: Cell<bool>, revision: Cell<u64>, lookup_cache: RefCell<Option<(String, String, Vec<Value>, Option<Value>)>> }
impl<'a> GuardedRows<'a> {
    pub(crate) fn new(raw: &'a dyn Rows) -> Self { Self { raw, remaining: Cell::new(AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT), authorizing: Cell::new(false), reference_prepaid: Cell::new(false), revision: Cell::new(0), lookup_cache: RefCell::new(None) } }
    pub(super) fn revision(&self) -> u64 { self.revision.get() }
    pub(super) fn remaining(&self) -> usize { self.remaining.get() }
    fn limit(&self, table: &str, requested: usize) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.authorizing.get() || self.reference_prepaid.get() { return Ok(requested); }
        if self.remaining.get() == 0 { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(requested.min(self.remaining.get()))
    }
    fn charge(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() && !self.reference_prepaid.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get().saturating_sub(fetched)); }
    }
    /// Return the walk allowance of a fetched row the store stepped over as
    /// expired: such rows are absent, so they cannot exhaust a read's walk.
    pub(super) fn refund(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get() + fetched); }
    }
    pub(crate) fn get(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.get(table, id)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    /// A key-only witness still consumes the shared physical authorization allowance.
    pub(crate) fn index_head(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir) -> Result<Option<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let key = self.raw.index_head(table, index, prefix, bounds, dir)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(key.is_some()));
        Ok(key)
    }
    pub(crate) fn lookup(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, index: &Index, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — repeated unchanged points share decoded storage,
        // while every logical read still prepays its complete rule.
        if let Some((cached_table, cached_index, cached_key, row)) = &*self.lookup_cache.borrow()
            && cached_table == table && cached_index == name && cached_key == key { return Ok(row.clone()); }
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.lookup(table, name, index, key)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        *self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some((table.into(), name.into(), key.to_vec(), row.clone()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub(crate) fn scan_selected(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, fields: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan_selected(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, fields)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan_metered(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = InternalLedger::with_meter(meter, |meter, _| {
            self.raw.scan_metered(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted, _authorization, meter)
        })?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn count_prefix(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], limit: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        // This scalar adapter is used only by non-expiring, non-private
        // caps. It fetches no candidate rows and keeps its declared cap bound.
        self.raw.count_prefix(table, name, prefix, limit)
    }

    pub(crate) fn cap_count(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.cap_count(table, by, group) }

    pub(crate) fn sequence_value(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, parent: &str, id: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.sequence_value(parent, id) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_sequence_floor(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, parent: &str, id: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_sequence_floor(parent, id) }
    pub(crate) fn set_sequence(&self, parent: &str, id: &str, value: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.raw.set_sequence(parent, id, value) }
    pub(crate) fn put(&self, name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.revision.set(self.revision.get().wrapping_add(1)); self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take(); self.raw.put(name, table, row, old) }
    pub(crate) fn delete(&self, name: &str, table: &Table, old: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.revision.set(self.revision.get().wrapping_add(1)); self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take(); self.raw.delete(name, table, old)
    }
    pub(crate) fn track_time_audience(
        &self,
        name: &str,
        table: &Table,
        id: &str,
        row: Option<&Value>,
        now: i64,
        published: bool,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.track_time_audience(name, table, id, row, now, published)
    }
    pub(crate) fn time_audience_pending(&self, name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.time_audience_pending(name, id) }
    pub(crate) fn time_audience_published(&self, name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.time_audience_published(name, id) }
    pub(crate) fn holds_assets(&self) -> bool { self.raw.holds_assets() }
    pub(crate) fn widen_change_evidence(&self) { self.raw.widen_change_evidence() }
    /// Draw a schema-fixed number of rows from the request's physical
    /// allowance up front, so later authorization work exhausts it at a point
    /// independent of the rows a delete's actions reach (LLP 3000.039 §4.2).
    pub(crate) fn draw(&self, table: &str, rows: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let rows = usize::try_from(rows).unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
        if self.remaining.get() < rows { self.remaining.set(0); return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Delete }); }
        self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get() - rows);
        Ok(())
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — reserved action hooks spend the physical draw once; ordinary root probes remain outside this scope.
    pub(super) fn reference_prepaid(&self, prepaid: bool) -> bool { self.reference_prepaid.replace(prepaid) }
    pub(crate) fn asset(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Asset>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.asset(id) }
    pub(crate) fn claim_asset(&self, id: &str, by: &str, table: &str, row: &str, column: &str) -> Result<Asset, RuntimeError> { self.raw.claim_asset(id, by, table, row, column) }
    pub(crate) fn release_asset(&self, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] table: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] row_id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] column: &str, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] { self.raw.release_asset(id, table, row_id, column, now) } #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))] { self.raw.release_asset(id, now) } }
    pub(crate) fn effect_once(&self, effect: &str, key: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.effect_once(effect, key) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_id_after(&self, table: &str, after: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_id_after(table, after) }
    pub(crate) fn has_provisional(&self, table: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.has_provisional(table) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_groups(&self, table: &str, fields: &[String]) -> Result<Option<Vec<Vec<crate::value::Value>>>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_groups(table, fields) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn search_retained_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.search_retained_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn retained_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.retained_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn retained_group(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.retained_group(table, index, prefix) }
    /// Device acquisition facts carry no row payload, like retained_row.
    pub(crate) fn current_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.current_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn current_group(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.current_group(table, index, prefix) }
    pub(crate) fn recording_covered(&self, table: &str, row: &Value, viewer: &str, now: i64, scope: &Authorization, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { InternalLedger::with_meter(meter, |meter, _| {
            self.raw.recording_covered(table, row, viewer, now, scope, meter)
        }) }
    pub(crate) fn acquired_boundary(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<AcquiredBoundary>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.acquired_boundary(table, index, prefix) }
    pub(crate) fn prefetch_enabled(&self) -> bool { self.raw.prefetch_enabled() }
    #[allow(dead_code)] // Used by the native prefetch planner; absent on web.
    pub(crate) fn prefetch(&self, requests: &[PrefetchRequest]) { self.raw.prefetch(requests) }
}

pub(super) struct PrepaidState {
    mark: crate::meter::MeterMark,
    ceilings: Ceilings,
    cut: Cut,
    authorizing: bool,
    nested: bool,
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — declaration-only scratch is admitted before any
    // candidate/witness access. The logical heap retains only disclosed output.
    pub(super) fn authorized_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, true, work)
    }

    /// Count certification follows an admitted member and source-group verdict.
    /// Nested authorization still shares any enclosing physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn disclosed_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, false, work)
    }

    pub(super) fn reserved_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }


    /// Ordinary data reads keep their existing meter and cut semantics. This
    /// scope grants storage access without disabling or restoring any charge.
    /// Hidden authorization must use authorized_scan's prepaid reservation.
    pub(crate) fn metered_scan<T>(&mut self, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        scan(self, &Authorization::internal())
    }

    /// A rule whose logical allowance the caller has already charged, possibly
    /// to a cut, still evaluates its witnesses under the physical allowance;
    /// the caller decides what its cut means for the answer (LLP 3000.014 §5).
    pub(super) fn prepaid_witnesses<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || !self.context.is_system());
        let result = work(self, &Authorization::internal());
        self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — admission depends on declared bounds, never on
    // occupants. Private scratch cannot cut the caller's prepaid scan.
    pub(crate) fn authorized_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.prepaid_scan(allowance, true, scan)
    }

    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
    /// meter; they do no authorization traversal. A nested scope cannot opt
    /// out of its enclosing authorization's physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn prepaid_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        // This meter is the device's separate physical-work budget. Its
        // caller maps exhaustion to uncertainty, never a logical E_BOUND.
        if self.device_work_active {
            let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system()));
            let result = scan(self, &Authorization::internal());
            self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
            return result;
        }
        for &(kind, amount) in allowance {
            if !self.read_charge(kind, amount)? { return Ok(T::default()); }
        }
        if self.read_capped() { return Ok(T::default()); }
        let scope = Authorization { probes: Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes.saturating_sub(self.meter.used.rule_probes) };
        let state = self.enter_prepaid(physical);
        let result = scan(self, &scope);
        self.leave_prepaid(state, result.as_ref().err());
        if scope.probes.get() > 0 { self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, scope.probes.get())?; }
        result
    }

    // Kept out of the generic closure wrapper: every protected access shares
    // the same scope transition instead of instantiating its restoration code.
    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn enter_prepaid(&mut self, physical: bool) -> PrepaidState {
        let nested = self.caller_heap_ceiling.is_some();
        self.caller_heap_ceiling.get_or_insert((self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes, self.meter.used.heap_bytes));
        PrepaidState {
            mark: self.meter.mark(),
            ceilings: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.ceilings, Ceilings::unbounded()),
            cut: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.cut, Cut::Abort),
            authorizing: self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system())),
            nested,
        }
    }

    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn leave_prepaid(&mut self, state: PrepaidState, error: Option<&RuntimeError>) {
        self.tx.authorizing.set(state.authorizing);
        self.meter.restore(state.mark);
        self.meter.ceilings = state.ceilings;
        self.meter.cut = state.cut;
        if !state.nested { self.caller_heap_ceiling = None; }
        if let Some(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::HeapBytes, site, .. }) = error {
            self.meter.capped_at.get_or_insert((MeterKind::HeapBytes, site.clone()));
        }
    }

}

#[cfg(all(test, feature = "sqlite"))]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Store, meter::Meter, schema::Schema};

    #[test]
    fn job_authority_edges_clear_raw_cache_but_keep_the_allowance() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &schema, Context::system(), Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort), 0);
        runner.tx.remaining.set(17);
        *runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        let child = runner.job_read_child(Context::new("user:alice", Default::default()));
        assert!(child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(child.tx.remaining(), 17);
        child.tx.remaining.set(13);
        *child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        runner.finish_job_read_child(child);
        assert!(runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(runner.tx.remaining(), 13);
    }

    #[test]
    fn raw_write_fetches_reserve_before_even_an_absent_candidate() {
        use crate::{
            Value,
            rows::MemoryRows, schema::{ColumnType, FieldAuthority, public_table},
        };
        let rows = MemoryRows::new();
        let mut schema = Schema::default();
        let mut table = public_table([("id".into(),ColumnType::Id),("owner".into(),ColumnType::Principal),
            ("secret".into(),ColumnType::Text { max: 1000000 })].into(), Default::default());
        table.indexes.insert("byId".into(),crate::schema::Index::of(&["id"],true));
        table.written_by.insert("secret".into(),FieldAuthority { owner:"owner".into(),audience:false });
        schema.tables.insert("records".into(),table);
        for lookup in [false,true] {
            let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),
                Meter::new(Ceilings {heap_bytes:32,..Default::default()},Cut::Abort),0);
            let result = if lookup {
                let index=&schema.tables["records"].indexes["byId"];
                runner.lookup_stored("records","byId",index,&[Value::Id("absent".into())],false)
            } else { runner.metered_scan(|runner, scope|runner.fetch(scope,"records","absent")) };
            assert!(matches!(result,Err(RuntimeError::Bound {kind:MeterKind::HeapBytes,..})),"lookup={lookup}: {result:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn scratch_keeps_only_the_fixed_peak_on_success_and_failure() {
        let rows = crate::rows::MemoryRows::new();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        for hidden in [0,1000000] {
            for refuses in [false,true] {
                let mut runner=Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),Meter::new(Ceilings::default(),Cut::Abort),0);
                let result=runner.authorized_scratch(4096,&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,2)],|runner,_| {
                    runner.meter.heap(hidden)?;
                    if refuses { Err(RuntimeError::Input("internal test error".into())) } else { Ok(()) }
                });
                assert_eq!(result.is_err(),refuses);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.heap_bytes,0);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.heap_peak,4096);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.rule_probes,2);
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn allowance_alone_decides_admission_and_every_exit_keeps_it() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        for cut in [Cut::Cap, Cut::Abort] {
            for hidden_work in [0, 1, 8193] {
                for refuses in [false, true] {
                    let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &schema, Context::new("alice", Default::default()), Meter::new(Ceilings { examined: 2, rule_probes: 2, heap_bytes: 8, ..Default::default() }, cut), 0);
                    let result = runner.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, 2), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, 2)], |runner, _| {
                        runner.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, hidden_work)?;
                        runner.meter.charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, hidden_work)?;
                        runner.meter.heap(hidden_work * 1024)?;
                        if refuses { Err(RuntimeError::Rule { table: "private".into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Insert }) }
                        else { Ok(()) }
                    });
                    assert_eq!(result.is_err(), refuses);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.examined, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.rule_probes, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.heap_bytes, 0);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.heap_peak, 0);
                    assert!(runner.meter.capped_at.is_none());
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.ceilings.examined, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.cut, cut);
                    let result = runner.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, 1)], |_, _| -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { panic!("admission must precede all occupant access") });
                    if cut == Cut::Abort { assert!(matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::Examined, actual: 3, ceiling: 2, .. }))); }
                    else { assert!(result.is_ok()); assert!(runner.meter.capped_at.is_some()); }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Standalone physical accounting has its own meter. Its token is borrowed
/// only for `run`, never constructible on a caller ledger or returned by it.
pub struct InternalLedger(crate::meter::Meter);
pub struct InternalMeter<'a>(&'a mut Meter);
impl InternalMeter<'_> {
    pub fn heap(&mut self, bytes: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.0.heap(bytes) }
    pub fn charge(&mut self, kind: MeterKind, amount: u64) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.0.charge(kind, amount) }
    pub fn at(&mut self, site: &str) { self.0.at(site); }
    pub fn step(&mut self, steps: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.0.step(steps) }
    /// Release temporary allocations without exposing the saved heap counter.
    pub fn scratch<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> T) -> T {
        let mark = self.0.mark();
        let result = work(self);
        self.0.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }
    pub fn examined_room(&self, rows: usize) -> bool { self.0.room(MeterKind::Examined, rows as u64) }
    pub(crate) fn room(&self, kind: MeterKind, amount: u64) -> bool { self.0.room(kind, amount) }
    pub(crate) fn guarded(&self) -> u64 { self.0.scratch }
    pub(crate) fn add_scratch(&mut self, bytes: u64) -> u64 { self.0.scratch = self.0.scratch.saturating_add(bytes); self.0.scratch }
}
impl InternalLedger {
    pub(crate) fn with_meter<T>(meter: &mut crate::meter::Meter, work: impl FnOnce(&mut InternalMeter<'_>, &Authorization) -> T,
    ) -> T {
        work(&mut InternalMeter(meter), &Authorization::internal())
    }
    pub fn new(meter: crate::meter::Meter) -> Self { Self(meter) }
    pub fn run<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut InternalMeter<'_>, &Authorization) -> T) -> T {
        work(&mut InternalMeter(&mut self.0), &Authorization::internal())
    }
}

#[cfg(all(test,feature="sqlite"))]
mod row_view_tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Schema, meter::Meter, rows::MemoryRows};
    #[test]
    fn internal_work_cannot_change_reserved_receipt_or_cut() {
        let rows=MemoryRows::new(); let schema=Schema::default();
        for hidden in [0,1,17<<20] {
            for error in [false,true] {
                let mut r=Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),Meter::new(Ceilings::default(),Cut::Cap),0);
                let out=r.authorized_scratch(8192,&[(MeterKind::Examined,1),(MeterKind::Steps,1)],|r,_| {
                    r.meter.heap(hidden)?; r.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined,hidden)?;
                    if error {Err(RuntimeError::Input("internal test error".into()))} else {Ok(())}
                });
                assert_eq!(out.is_err(),error);
                assert_eq!(r.meter.used.examined,1); assert_eq!(r.meter.used.steps,1);
                assert_eq!(r.meter.used.heap_bytes,0); assert_eq!(r.meter.heap_peak,8192);
                assert!(r.meter.capped_at.is_none()); assert_eq!(r.meter.ceilings,Ceilings::default());
            }
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl<'a> Runner<'a> {
    /// Fresh read state over this exact transaction; move the guarded allowance
    /// with the meter so nested queries cannot reset authorization work.
    pub(super) fn job_read_child(&mut self, context: super::Context) -> Runner<'a> {
        self.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take();
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort));
        let mut child = Runner::new(self.tx.raw, self.schema, context, meter, self.seq);
        std::mem::swap(&mut child.tx, &mut self.tx);
        child.job_read_scope = 2;
        child
    }
    pub(super) fn finish_job_read_child(&mut self, mut child: Runner<'a>) {
        child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take();
        std::mem::swap(&mut child.tx, &mut self.tx);
        self.meter = child.meter;
        self.read_tables.extend(child.read_tables);
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.028#3.3.1 — decoration has separate coverage, never separate
// resource or authorization allowances. Move both, including exhausted counters.
impl<'a> Runner<'a> {
    pub(super) fn decoration_child(&mut self) -> Result<Runner<'a>, RuntimeError> {
        self.meter.heap(1024 + self.context.args.values().map(super::runner::expression_heap).sum::<u64>())?;
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        self.meter.heap(self.context.search_retained_order.as_ref().map_or(0, |(keys, _)| keys.len() as u64 * 8)
            + self.context.search_retained_candidates.iter().map(|(id, _, _)| 64 + id.len() as u64).sum::<u64>())?;
        let placeholder = Meter::new(self.meter.ceilings, self.meter.cut);
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, placeholder);
        let mut child = Runner::new(self.tx.raw, self.schema, self.context.clone(), meter, self.seq);
        std::mem::swap(&mut child.tx, &mut self.tx);
        child.device = self.device;
        child.meter.cut = Cut::Abort;
        Ok(child)
    }
    pub(super) fn finish_decoration_child(&mut self, mut child: Runner<'a>) {
        child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take();
        std::mem::swap(&mut child.tx, &mut self.tx);
        child.meter.cut = self.meter.cut;
        self.meter = child.meter;
        self.read_tables.extend(child.read_tables);
        // Decoration expiry invalidates the value, but never changes freshness
        // or the page's completeness. Re-evaluation substitutes null after expiry.
        self.decoration_expiry = child.next_expiry.get();
    }
}

llp/reviews/3000.007-w14a-implementation.recheck-2.grok.md
None of them generates random programs, and none compares one case across
every executor. Stage 1 adds those two things and reuses the rest.

**Where it lives.** A new workspace crate, `snapback4-difftest`, with three
parts: the generator and harness as a library, a runner binary for
scheduled runs, and a fixed-seed test target. It adds no dependency to the
shipped crates or to the wasm device.

**The generator.** From a seed, it produces Quarry source text, not IR, so
that the parser and binder are under test. Each program comes with its
schema, rows, principals, arguments, clock and meter budget. It starts with
a core fragment and widens later:

- tables with int, text, id, bool and nullable columns; unique keys and
  indexes;
- rules: allow, deny, owner equals viewer, and an `exists` witness;
- residency: public sync, sync to owner, and online only;
- point reads, index scans with filters and order, bounded reads and pages,
  counts and simple aggregates;
- insert, upsert, update and delete operations;
- expressions with integers near the i53 bounds, text outside the Basic
  Multilingual Plane, short-circuit operators, null assertions and pure
  helper calls.

Every generated program must compile. A generated program the binder
refuses is either a generator bug, or a refusal the generator records as
intended.

**The oracles.** Each case is compared on the full outcome record of LLP
3000.009 §11.1: data, completeness, cut sites, cursors, every meter field,
tables touched, next expiry, and refusals with their sites. It is compared
across:

1. the engine on SQLite against the engine on Postgres, with prefetch on
   and off (Postgres runs where a cluster is available, as
   `scripts/test-postgres.sh` provides one);
2. the uncut run against a run with a meter cut forced at each charge
   position. Each cut must report a cut, and continuing from its cursor must
   reach the uncut answer;
3. the program against meaning-preserving rewrites of itself;
4. the server against a device that has acquired the same rows, on reads
   the device reports complete. This is a later slice: it needs the
   simulator's acquisition path.

**Rewrite costs (2026-10-12).** Rewrites compare every semantic field, but
omit meter fields when the rewrite changes executed work; the report counts
5:**Systems:** Quarry (`snapback4-lang`: parser, binder, emitters), the program IR and interpreter (`snapback4-core`), the device interpreter (`snapback4-device`), the SQLite and Postgres stores, a new differential-testing crate, a later Lean 4 project, scheduled verification

416:### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes
2777:### 18.5 A guess costs a command, not a loop; Trending is on the card
  never by a mapping reconstructed from column names or types; principal
  partitions are tagged so no principal's name collides with `public`; a
  commit is delivered whole however large; a grant that ends inside its
  own commit delivers nothing; a delivered aggregate's audience is its
  source's. Horizons bound acquisition, not holdings: a device holds what
  was delivered, and a local read past the horizon says `complete: false`.
- **2026-09-12 — completeness is a class, not a count (lead, after the
  meter review).** As Snapback 2 (LLP 1001 §3): a query with a scan the
  prefix does not decide is `complete: false` whatever the meter found,
  and a capped page's `next` continues from its last visible row or ends
  on an empty page; the meter's numbers, its cut site and a refusal's
  count stay off the viewer wire; a hidden row costs what a miss costs and
  a hoisted-false range what an empty one costs. The device runs the same
  hoist; its `complete` speaks of the horizon and the meter alone, since a
  device holds only its partition.

  **2026-09-15 — W7c development inspection (author's lane brief).**
  Loopback `dev` may return the existing engine counters on successful reads
  explicitly requesting `x-snapback-meter: 1`; the online client exposes this
  as `createClient({ meter: true })` and optional `Read.meter`. The counters
  describe the returned server answer, including a remembered watch answer.
- **2026-09-13 — hidden rows stay hidden at write and replay boundaries
  (security review).** A unique conflict with a row the caller cannot read is
  the generic rule refusal. Audience insertion is admitted only when the
  caller already has the matching grant; audience keys cannot be changed to
  acquire one. The private mutation log may retain old operator data, but sync
  catch-up re-evaluates the current row and emits only its current readable
  image, or a deletion for a row that disappeared or expired. Previously
  delivered device data remains a property of that device.

- **2026-10-12 — a third asset kind, `file`, under the same contract
  (author, approving LLP 3000.038).** A document column (`file <=N`, with an
  optional allow-list of media types Snapback can verify) keeps one home,
  serving by the home row's read rule and the release/sweep lifetime above.
  The upload declares its type and filename (`?as=file&name=`); the server
  checks the declaration against the bytes and never stores a script-bearing
  type. A file is only ever served as an attachment (`nosniff`, a
  sandboxing CSP); `once` does not apply; nothing is transcoded.

- **2026-09-13 — assets are staged, bounded and viewer-private (security
  review).** Server IDs use operating-system or Web Crypto entropy. Upload
  bytes and asset deletion run outside the engine mutex, with bounded slots
  and a generation/session check before commit. A once asset is consumable by
  its placed recipient, while its unplaced uploader retains preview access;
  ranged reads cannot bypass consumption. Image dimensions, total pixels and
  video duration are capped. Unsatisfiable ranges return 416 and every asset
  response is `private, no-store`, so a browser cache cannot cross principals.

- **2026-09-13 — optimize the production server for speed.**
  The production Cargo profile uses optimization level 3 after an
  identical-source comparison with the former size setting. On the recorded
  100,000-row catalog, the speed build has lower median p50 in eight of eight
  HTTP cases and lower p95 in seven; continuation-page p95 worsens. The Linux
  ARM64 executable grows from 3,296,936 B to 5,787,152 B, below the recorded
None of them generates random programs, and none compares one case across
every executor. Stage 1 adds those two things and reuses the rest.

**Where it lives.** A new workspace crate, `snapback4-difftest`, with three
parts: the generator and harness as a library, a runner binary for
scheduled runs, and a fixed-seed test target. It adds no dependency to the
shipped crates or to the wasm device.

**The generator.** From a seed, it produces Quarry source text, not IR, so
that the parser and binder are under test. Each program comes with its
schema, rows, principals, arguments, clock and meter budget. It starts with
a core fragment and widens later:

- tables with int, text, id, bool and nullable columns; unique keys and
  indexes;
- rules: allow, deny, owner equals viewer, and an `exists` witness;
- residency: public sync, sync to owner, and online only;
- point reads, index scans with filters and order, bounded reads and pages,
  counts and simple aggregates;
- insert, upsert, update and delete operations;
- expressions with integers near the i53 bounds, text outside the Basic
  Multilingual Plane, short-circuit operators, null assertions and pure
  helper calls.

Every generated program must compile. A generated program the binder
refuses is either a generator bug, or a refusal the generator records as
intended.

**The oracles.** Each case is compared on the full outcome record of LLP
3000.009 §11.1: data, completeness, cut sites, cursors, every meter field,
tables touched, next expiry, and refusals with their sites. It is compared
across:

1. the engine on SQLite against the engine on Postgres, with prefetch on
   and off (Postgres runs where a cluster is available, as
   `scripts/test-postgres.sh` provides one);
2. the uncut run against a run with a meter cut forced at each charge
   position. Each cut must report a cut, and continuing from its cursor must
   reach the uncut answer;
3. the program against meaning-preserving rewrites of itself;
4. the server against a device that has acquired the same rows, on reads
   the device reports complete. This is a later slice: it needs the
   simulator's acquisition path.

**Rewrite costs (2026-10-12).** Rewrites compare every semantic field, but
omit meter fields when the rewrite changes executed work; the report counts
these omissions. In particular, commuting an expression that refuses may
reach the failing operand before evaluating the other one. LANG2's merged
run found `a = (b + b)` refusing the same overflow with six versus five
steps after commutation. The retained case
`cases/found/rewrite-commute--metered--E_OVERFLOW` pins this harness repair
mod common;
use common::*;
use snapback4_core::{Context, WriteState};
use snapback4_device::{device::Device,store::DeviceStore};
use serde_json::json;
const SOURCE:&str="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  unique parent\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nfn label(p: parents): p.label\nmutation add(id: id, child: id, title: text <=20):\n  p = insert parents { id, label: title }\n  insert kids { id: child, parent: p }\n  return label(p)\nmutation clash(id: id, parent: parents):\n  insert parents { id: 'rolledback', label: 'temporary' }\n  insert kids { id, parent }\nquery parents(c: cursor ?):\n  return parents first 20 by .id after c\n";
fn run<H:Host>(host:&mut H){
 let (mut e,b)=compile(SOURCE);
 let mut d=host.open("qdiag",Some(b)).unwrap();
 for (i,page) in snapshot_pages(&mut e,"alice",10).iter().enumerate(){d.apply(page,i==0).unwrap();}
 let args=json!({"id":"p","child":"k","title":"label"});
 d.predict("add","alice",&args,100,vec![],1).unwrap();
 let actual=e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("p")),("child",id("k")),("title",snapback4_core::Value::String("label".into()))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(actual.state,WriteState::Sent { result:snapback4_core::Value::String(ref s),.. } if s=="label"),"{actual:?}");
 let row=e.store().rows("kids").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
 assert_eq!(row.field("parent").unwrap(),&id("p"));
 let predicted=d.predict("clash","alice",&json!({"id":"other","parent":"p"}),100,vec![],2).unwrap_err();
 assert_eq!(predicted.code(),"E_UNIQUE","{predicted:?}");
 let conflict=e.mutate("clash",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("other")),("parent",id("p"))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(conflict.state,WriteState::Failed { ref why,.. } if why.code=="E_UNIQUE" && why.message.contains("unique parent") && !why.message.contains("$intent")),"{conflict:?}");
 assert_eq!(e.store().rows("parents").unwrap().len(),1,"the earlier write rolled back");
 assert_eq!(query(&mut d),json!([{"id":"p","label":"label","pending":true}]),"device refusal rolled back");
 host.close("qdiag",d);
}
fn query<S:DeviceStore>(d:&mut Device<S>)->serde_json::Value{snapback4_device::call::call(d,json!({"op":"query","name":"parents","viewer":"alice","args":{"c":null},"now":100}))["ok"]["data"].clone()}
#[test]fn qdiag_memory(){run(&mut Memory::default());}
#[test]fn qdiag_sqlite(){run(&mut Sqlite::new("wave11-qdiag"));}
use snapback4_difftest::{generate,execute,lean};
#[test]
fn generated_contextual_sources_match_native_and_lean(){
    for seed in 0..32 {
        let (case,implicit,explicit)=generate::qdiag(seed);
        let a=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&implicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        let b=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&explicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        assert_eq!(a,b,"seed {seed}");
        for source in [&implicit,&explicit] {
            let checked=lean::compare(&case,source).unwrap();
            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict,snapback4_difftest::oracle::Verdict::Pass),"seed {seed}: {:?}",checked.verdict);
        }
    }
}
//! Compare implicit/explicit row-reference worlds through the simulator's
//! wire, receipts, stream, settlement and device-reopen observation harness.
#[path="common/write_observation.rs"] mod observation;
use snapback4_core::Engine;
use serde_json::json;
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
#[test]
fn contextual_projection_preserves_every_published_observation(){
 let source="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nmutation create(id: id, kid: id):\n  parent = insert parents { id, label:'parent' }\n  insert kids { id:kid, parent }\n  return parent.id\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return kids first 10 by .id after c\n";
 let mut a=engine(source);let mut b=engine(&source.replace("id:kid, parent","id:kid, parent:parent.id").replace("5MB","5000000"));
 let (_,a)=observation::observe(&mut a,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 let (_,b)=observation::observe(&mut b,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 a.assert_same(&b);
}

+/// Wave-11 contextual reference family. Keep the sources independent of IR;
+/// the explicit spelling is a metamorphic oracle for the contextual spelling.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — inserted/lookup snapshots project only in reference contexts.
+pub fn qdiag(seed:u64) -> (Case,String,String) {
+    let mut case=base(seed);
+    case.program.body=Body::Block(Block { stmts:vec![],ret:Expr::Bool(true) });
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear(); case.budget=Ceilings::default();
+    case.args=json!({"key":"new-parent","k2":"new-child","bucket":0,"c":null,"n":null,"a":0,"b":1});
+    let mut rng=Rng::new(seed);
+    let (owner,visible)=(if rng.pick(2)==0 {"alice"} else {"bob"},rng.pick(2)==0);
+    if rng.pick(3)==0 { case.rows.push(("kids".into(),json!({"id":"old-child","item":"new-parent","owner":owner}))); }
+    let read=if visible {"allow"} else {".owner = viewer"};
+    let implicit=format!("table items:\n  sibling: items ? on delete keep\n  unique sibling\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=64\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  item: items on delete keep\n  owner: principal\n  unique item\n  read <- {read}\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation run(key: id, k2: id, bucket: int, c: cursor ?, n: int ?, a: int, b: int):\n  item = insert items {{ id:key, owner:viewer, label:'parent' }}\n  insert kids {{ id:k2, item, owner:viewer }}\n  seen = items[items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return seen.id\nquery observe(key: id, k2: id):\n  return {{ item:items[key], child:kids[k2] }}\n");
+    let explicit=implicit.replace("id:k2, item, owner","id:k2, item:item.id, owner").replace("items[items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND]","items[(items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND).id]");
+    (case,implicit,explicit)
+}

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;
### 18.5 A guess costs a command, not a loop; Trending is on the card

The mechanical set (§5.5, §8) grows by the loops the 0.2.9 lane paid: the
index a read needs (`E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN` naming a `by` line, inserted into the
table's index block in whichever file declares the table); `once` written
on an argument (dropped; the modifier belongs to the column); a field
repeated in a `*` shape (dropped); the parentheses an operation with no
arguments needs (inserted). A mechanical diagnostic prints `check --fix
applies it` after its rewrite, so the command is discovered where the loop
would have been. The feeds card gains the Trending recipe of §5.3 (the
trailing day sought up to the ceiling, sorted, sliced, `complete: false`
past it) beside the maintained ranking. A helper parameter typed by a
table: where helpers are inlined per call and the argument is a row, the
parameter binds as the row; otherwise the diagnostic on a field access names
the point lookup and the shape parameter and no form is added — the lane
reports which.

Built (lane CU5): helpers are inlined per call, so a row passed to a
table-typed parameter binds as the row, and a reference keeps its meaning.

**2026-09-14 — built by lane W5.** The pinned-prefix verdict charges the reservation's static probe count once on either outcome, independent of short-circuit answers; the common attempt and verdict-before-candidate order remain. The shared helper proves all seven meters and complete observations equal for the eight denied pairs and eight true-verdict controls, on both device stores; two-probe rules charge 2 with room and reach 8193 after the 8191-probe prelude, refusing both worlds with `E_BOUND`.

integration commit `4582f8421` blind, read-only, against `518891fd1`:
[GPT](./reviews/3000.007-catch-up-landing.gpt.md) NOT READY, twelve
findings; [Grok](./reviews/3000.007-catch-up-landing.grok.md) READY WITH
CHANGES, four. A finding one family raised was a hypothesis: each fix lane

    /// A system read of an expiring table takes the store path, so the store

    #[cfg_attr(not(feature = "sqlite"), allow(dead_code))]
    fn live_store_scan(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();

            if metered { crate::rows::word_heap::scan_open(meter, name, index, prefix, bounds, scope)?; }
            Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(crate::rows::word_heap::StoreGuard::open(meter, super::read::EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD))

    /// One fetched candidate's expiry, rule and field verdict, inside an
    /// internal scope. Unadmitted rule work is an unknown fact on a device.
    fn candidate_verdict(&mut self, scope: &super::authorized_scan::Authorization, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], mut row: Value, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
        let provisional = self.is_provisional(name,&row)?;
        let needs_rule = !self.context.is_system() && (!trusted || provisional);
        if needs_rule && !rule_prepaid && !self.device_work_active && !scope.admit_probes(super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read)) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.020#3.2 — unresolved authority is unknown, not an absent contribution.
            if self.device { self.unknown_fact(name, index, prefix, "source rule admission"); }
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if trusted && !provisional && self.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| m.parent_key.is_some()) {
            return Ok(Some(self.authorized_synthetic(table, row)));
        }
        self.authorize_image(name,row)
    }

    /// Cold certification has already admitted the member and entire source
    /// group, including a decoder page reserved before entering prepaid work. Every row
    /// still receives the ordinary expiry, rule and field verdict below.
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — live count certification shares its transaction and authority.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(super) fn scan_acquisition_basis(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        const PAGE: usize = 32;
        assert!(!self.device);
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        let rows = self.disclosed_scratch(0, &[], |runner, scope| {
            let selected = runner.tx.scan(scope, name, index, prefix, bounds, crate::rows::Dir::Asc, PAGE)?;
            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(selected.len());
            for (mut row, key) in selected {
                crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                let provisional = runner.is_provisional(name, &row)?;
                if provisional && !runner.context.is_system() && !scope.admit_probes(super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read)) {
                    out.push((None, key)); continue;
                }
                let row = if !provisional && runner.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| m.parent_key.is_some()) {
                    Some(runner.authorized_synthetic(table, row))
                } else { runner.authorize_image(name, row)? };
                out.push((row, key));
            }
        })
    }

    /// Whether the viewer may `effect` the row. The system principal is the
    /// store's owner and passes every rule; validation and constraints
    /// still run for it.
    pub(crate) fn check_rule(&mut self, table_name: &str, effect: Effect, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if effect != Effect::Read && self.timeline_may_write(table_name, effect) { return Ok(true); }
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.is_provisional(table_name, next)? { return self.provisional_readable(table_name, next); }
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        // A device holds a synced table's rows because the server delivered
        // each under the read rule and withdraws what it revokes; what
        // `retain delivered-history` kept stays readable here (LLP 3000 §7).
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.trust_synced_read(table) {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7(a): an unnamed update rule judges old and next.
        let mut rule = table.rules.for_effect(effect).clone();
        if effect == Effect::Update && old.is_some() && table.judges_old_image() { rule = rule.old_and_next(); }
        if effect == Effect::Update && let Some(old) = old {
            for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
                if !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    rule.fold_untouched_field(field, &table.columns[field]);
                }
            }
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read { self.rule_dependencies(&rule); }
        let bytes = self.rule_scratch(&rule);
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&rule))], |runner, allowance| {
            let mut next = next.clone();
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut next, table);
            let old = old.map(|old| {
                let mut old = old.clone();
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut old, table);
                old
            });
            runner.evaluate_sequence_rule(allowance, table_name, &rule, effect, old.as_ref(), &next)
        }).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, effect));
        self.trace_rule_result(&result, table_name, effect);
        result
    }

    /// A row by id, materialized, without a rule check.
    pub(crate) fn fetch(&mut self, _allowance: &Authorization, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let found = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            self.live_lookup(_allowance, table_name, "byId", &table.indexes["byId"], &[Value::Id(id.into())], #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
        } else { self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, authority| {
            runner.tx.get(authority, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                        found.map(|mut row| {
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                row
            })
                    })
        })? };
        if self.device && (table.sync.is_none() || (found.is_none() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()))) { self.unknown_fact(table_name, "byId", &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())], "write target"); }
        match found {
            Some(row) if !self.inactive(table_name, &row)? => Ok(Some(row)),
            _ => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// A server write may address an unopened row without making that image
    /// readable. Fixed decode admission prevents its payload size from
    /// selecting a public meter result.
    pub(crate) fn fetch_write_target(
        &mut self,
        allowance: &Authorization,
        table_name: &str,
        id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .is_none_or(|table| table.not_before.is_none())
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let found = self.authorized_scratch(
            crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table),
            &[],
            |runner, _| {
                runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                    found.map(|mut row| {
                        materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                        row
                    })
    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
    /// meter; they do no authorization traversal. A nested scope cannot opt
    /// out of its enclosing authorization's physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn prepaid_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        // This meter is the device's separate physical-work budget. Its
        // caller maps exhaustion to uncertainty, never a logical E_BOUND.
        if self.device_work_active {
            let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system()));
            let result = scan(self, &Authorization::internal());
            self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
            return result;
        }
        for &(kind, amount) in allowance {
            if !self.read_charge(kind, amount)? { return Ok(T::default()); }
        }
        if self.read_capped() { return Ok(T::default()); }
        let scope = Authorization { probes: Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes.saturating_sub(self.meter.used.rule_probes) };
        let state = self.enter_prepaid(physical);
        let result = scan(self, &scope);
        self.leave_prepaid(state, result.as_ref().err());
        if scope.probes.get() > 0 { self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, scope.probes.get())?; }
        result
    }

    // Kept out of the generic closure wrapper: every protected access shares
    // the same scope transition instead of instantiating its restoration code.
    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn enter_prepaid(&mut self, physical: bool) -> PrepaidState {
        let nested = self.caller_heap_ceiling.is_some();
        self.caller_heap_ceiling.get_or_insert((self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes, self.meter.used.heap_bytes));
        PrepaidState {
            mark: self.meter.mark(),
            ceilings: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.ceilings, Ceilings::unbounded()),
            cut: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.cut, Cut::Abort),
            authorizing: self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system())),
            nested,
        }
    }

    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn leave_prepaid(&mut self, state: PrepaidState, error: Option<&RuntimeError>) {
        self.tx.authorizing.set(state.authorizing);
        self.meter.restore(state.mark);
        self.meter.ceilings = state.ceilings;
        self.meter.cut = state.cut;
        if !state.nested { self.caller_heap_ceiling = None; }
        if let Some(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::HeapBytes, site, .. }) = error {
            self.meter.capped_at.get_or_insert((MeterKind::HeapBytes, site.clone()));
        }
    }

pub(super) fn call(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    let principal = match principal_of(&request, shared) { Ok(p) => p.0, Err(why) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": why })) };
    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(body) { Ok(Json::Object(p)) => Json::Object(p), _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "an action takes { id, args }") })) };
    let Some(id) = payload.get("id").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "mint an id on the client and reuse it when retrying this action") })); };
    let admitted = {
        let mut engine = producing_engine(shared);
        if let Err(why) = intent_store(&engine, &payload) {
            let metadata = response_head(&mut engine);
            drop(engine);
            return respond(request, 200, match metadata {
                Ok((store_id, head)) => json!({ "denied": why, "store_id": store_id, "head": head }),
                Err(why) => json!({ "denied": why }),
            });
        }
        let raw_args = payload.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}));
        match engine.replay_action(id, &principal, name, &raw_args, shared.now()) {
            Ok(Some(run)) => Ok((run, true)),
            Err(error) => Err(error),
            Ok(None) => {
                let args = engine.program(name).filter(|p| p.kind == ProgramKind::Action).ok_or_else(|| snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_ACTION_UNKNOWN", message: format!("no action {name} is declared") })
                    .and_then(|p| {
                        if let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&p.args,&raw_args) {Err(snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Input(why.message))}
                        else {arguments(p, &raw_args)}
                    });
                args.and_then(|args| {
                    let mut context = Context::new(&principal, args).with_wire_args(raw_args).at(shared.now());
                    if let Ok(token) = crate::auth::bearer_token(&request) { context = context.with_session(token); }
                    engine.request_action(name, id, context)
                })
            }
        }
    };
    let (mut run, replayed) = match admitted { Ok(run) => run, Err(error) => { let why = failure(&error); return respond(request, if why.retryable { 503 } else { 200 }, json!({ "denied": why })); } };
    shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(body) {
        Ok(v) => v,
        Err(_) => {
            return respond_timed(
                request,
                shared,
                400,
                json!({"phase":"refused","code":"E_FOLLOWING_INPUT"}),
            );
        }
    };
    // Lock order is engine then controller. Authentication is checked again at
    // the same engine incarnation that owns the source read transaction.
    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());
    if let Err(r) = mutation_authority(&request, shared, &engine, &principal) {
        drop(engine);
        return respond_timed(request, shared, 401, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&r)}));
    }
    let Some(program) = engine.program(operation) else {
        drop(engine);
        return respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({"phase":"restart"}));
    };
    let Some(plan) = projection::plan(engine.schema(), program) else {
        drop(engine);
        return respond_timed(
            request,
            shared,
            200,
            json!({"phase":"refused","code":"E_FOLLOWING_DECLARATION"}),
        );
    };
    let binding = plan.binding;
    let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
    let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(Json::as_object) else {
        drop(engine);
        return respond_timed(
            request,
            shared,
            400,
            json!({"phase":"refused","code":"E_FOLLOWING_INPUT"}),
        );
    };
    if let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,&Json::Object(fields.clone())) {
        drop(engine);return respond_timed(request,shared,400,json!({"phase":"refused","code":"E_FOLLOWING_INPUT","message":why.message,"denied":why}));
    }
    for (name, value) in fields {
        let Some(value) = program
            .args
            .get(name)
            .and_then(|kind| wire::from_json(kind, value).ok())
        else {
            drop(engine);
            return respond_timed(
                request,
                shared,
                400,
                json!({"phase":"refused","code":"E_FOLLOWING_INPUT"}),
            );
        };
        args.insert(name.clone(), value);
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    match work(shared, true, 0) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules);
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    }
}

/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
    let service=if service_route {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
        }
    } else {
        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
        None
    };
    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };
    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
    let program = match program {
        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
    };
    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
        for (field, value) in fields {
            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
            }
        }
    }
    let mut admitted=lock(&shared.engine);
    crate::release_requests::stamp(admitted.generation());
    if let Some(service)=&service {
        if let Err(why)=super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&admitted,service) {drop(admitted);return refuse(request,403,why);}
    }
    let queued=admitted.enqueue_job(name,args,shared.now());
    drop(admitted);
    crate::release_requests::executed();
