//! Advisory mismatches between a program's caller gate and its table rule.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{ast::*, Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn seat(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
    let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. }=expr else { return None; };
    if op == "and" { return seat(left).or_else(|| seat(right)); }
    if op != "=" { return None; }
    let name = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Name { name,.. } if name == "viewer");
    let value = if name(left) { right.as_ref() } else if name(right) { left.as_ref() } else { return None; };
    match value { Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(s),.. } if s.starts_with("service:") => Some(s), _=>None }
}
fn fields(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<String> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>vec![f.clone()],
        RuleExpr::And(rs)|RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().flat_map(fields).collect(),
        _=>vec![]
    }
}
fn required_owners(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<String> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(parts)=>parts.iter().flat_map(required_owners).collect(),
        RuleExpr::Or(parts)=>{
            let Some(first)=parts.first() else {return vec![]};
            let mut common=required_owners(first);
            for part in &parts[1..] {let have=required_owners(part);common.retain(|f|have.contains(f));}
            common
        }
        other=>fields(other),
    }
}
fn admits_seat(rule: &RuleExpr, seat: &str) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Public { .. }=>true,
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>v.as_str().ok()==Some(seat),
        RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().any(|r|admits_seat(r,seat)),
        _=>false
    }
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — these notes state conditional argument requirements, never an impossibility theorem over dynamic inputs.
pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for table in &doc.tables {
            for column in &table.columns {
                if column.type_.kind==TypeKind::Principal && let Some(Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(value),..})=&column.default
                    && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p|p==value) {
                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(&column.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{}.{} defaults to bare persona '{value}', whose dev principal is 'dev:{value}'",table.name,column.name),format!("use `default 'dev:{value}'` for development, or supply the authenticated principal in the mutation for production")).guide("personas"));
                }
            }
        }
        for program in &doc.programs {
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query || program.native { continue; }
            body(catalog,schema,program,&program.body,None,&mut out);
        }
    }
    out
}
fn body(catalog:&Catalog,schema:&Schema,program:&ProgramDecl,statements:&[Stmt], mut gate:Option<String>,out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut lookups: BTreeMap<String,(String,Vec<String>)>=BTreeMap::new();
    for statement in statements {
        if let Stmt::Require { condition,code,site,.. }=statement {
            if let Some(seat)=seat(condition) { gate=Some(seat.into()); }
            for (alias,(table,owners)) in &lookups {
                let mut uses_owner=false; let mut copy=condition.clone();
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy,&mut |e| if let Expr::Member { base,name,.. }=e && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Name { name,.. } if name==alias) && owners.contains(name) { uses_owner=true; });
                if uses_owner && code != "NOT_FOUND" {
                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",format!("{}: callers who cannot read {table} get NOT_FOUND at `{alias}`'s lookup, never {code} from this require",program.name),
                        format!("put a caller authorization check before the lookup if {code} must win; otherwise keep NOT_FOUND as the privacy-preserving answer and test it (guide rules)")).guide("rules"));
                }
            }
        }
        if let Stmt::Bind { names,value:Expr::Assert { value,code,.. },.. }=statement && code=="NOT_FOUND" && let Expr::Read(read)=value.as_ref() {
            if let [name]=names.as_slice() && let Some(table)=schema.table(&read.table) {
                let owners=required_owners(&table.rules.read);
                if !owners.is_empty() { lookups.insert(name.clone(),(read.table.clone(),owners)); }
            }
        }
        let mut copy=vec![statement.clone()];
        // Inspect only this statement's expressions; nested control flow receives its own gate below.
        if !matches!(statement,Stmt::If { .. }|Stmt::For { .. }|Stmt::As { .. }) {
            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy,&mut |expr| {
                let Expr::Write { op,table,fields:payload,site,.. }=expr else { return; };
                let Some(def)=schema.table(table) else { return; };
                for field in payload.iter() {
                    let principal=catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(&field.name)).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Principal);
                    if principal && let Some(Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(value),.. })=&field.value
                        && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p| p==value) {
                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(&field.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{table}.{} stores bare persona '{value}', but its dev viewer is 'dev:{value}'",field.name),format!("write 'dev:{value}' for development, or an authenticated account principal in production; personas are names, not principals")).guide("personas"));
                    }
                }
                let Some(seat)=gate.as_deref() else { return; };
                let effect=match op { WriteOp::Insert=>"insert",WriteOp::Update=>"update", _=>return };
                let rule=if effect=="insert" { &def.rules.insert } else { &def.rules.update };
                if admits_seat(rule,seat) { return; }
                let others=required_owners(rule).into_iter().filter(|owner| payload.iter().find(|f|&f.name==owner).map_or(effect=="update",|f| {
                    let value=f.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(||Expr::Name { name:f.name.clone(),site:f.site.clone() });
                    !matches!(value,Expr::Name { ref name,.. } if name=="viewer") && !matches!(value,Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(ref s),.. } if s==seat)
                })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                if others.is_empty() { return; }
                out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_WRITE_DENIED",format!("{} admits only '{seat}', but {table}'s {effect} rule `{}` requires the written {} to be this caller; writing another principal's value refuses E_RULE",program.name,rule.quarry(),others.join(", ")),
                    format!("if this trusted seat should write other principals' rows, amend `{effect} <- {}` to `{effect} <- ({}) or viewer = '{seat}'`; the mutation require alone grants no table authority (guide deploy, first admin)",rule.quarry(),rule.quarry())).guide("rules"));
            });
        }
        match statement {
            Stmt::If { condition,then_body,else_body,.. }=> { body(catalog,schema,program,then_body,seat(condition).map(String::from).or_else(||gate.clone()),out); body(catalog,schema,program,else_body,gate.clone(),out); }
            Stmt::For { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,gate.clone(),out),
            Stmt::As { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,None,out),
            Stmt::Return { .. }|Stmt::Break { .. }|Stmt::Continue { .. }=>break,
            _=>{}
        }
    }
}

        }
        self.refuse_receipt_table(table)?;
        self.refuse_condition_write()?;
        self.refuse_timeline_write(table, effect)?;
        self.schema.refuse_private(table)?;
        if self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table} is a maintained aggregate; write its source rows instead")));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

//! Console aggregation stays on the evidence writer, outside request paths.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — one counted line per cause in a fixed one-second window.
use serde_json::Value;
use std::{collections::VecDeque, io::Write, time::{Duration, Instant}};

const WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
const LIMIT: usize = 32;
struct Entry { record: Value, count: u64, until: Instant }
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Console { entries: VecDeque<Entry>, detail: bool }

fn same_cause(a: &Value, b: &Value) -> bool {
    ["operation", "code", "table", "clause", "phase", "site", "executionSite", "generation", "hiddenBy", "preflight"]
        .iter().all(|key| a[key] == b[key])
}
impl Console {
    /// `dev --verbose` keeps the full evidence line; the default is one short line per cause.
    pub fn with_detail(detail: bool) -> Self { Self { entries: VecDeque::new(), detail } }
    pub fn wait(&self, now: Instant) -> Duration {
        self.entries.front().map_or(WINDOW, |entry| entry.until.saturating_duration_since(now))
    }
    pub fn push(&mut self, record: Value, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        self.push_at(record, Instant::now(), identity, out);
    }
    pub(super) fn push_at(&mut self, record: Value, now: Instant, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        self.flush_due(now, identity, out);
        if let Some(entry) = self.entries.iter_mut().find(|entry| same_cause(&entry.record, &record)) {
            entry.count = entry.count.saturating_add(1);
            return;
        }
        // Bounded even under distinct operations/causes: end the oldest window
        // early rather than losing its count or growing memory without bound.
        if self.entries.len() == LIMIT { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
        self.entries.push_back(Entry { record, count: 1, until: now + WINDOW });
    }
    pub fn flush_due(&mut self, now: Instant, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        while self.entries.front().is_some_and(|entry| entry.until <= now) { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
    }
    pub fn flush_all(&mut self, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        while !self.entries.is_empty() { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
    }
    fn emit_first(&mut self, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        let entry = self.entries.pop_front().unwrap();
        let r = entry.record;
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — a counted refusal remains one line beside lifecycle diagnostics.
        // Raw signal/thread-exit writes do not share Rust's stderr lock. Format
        // the complete line before touching the descriptor, so they cannot
        // splice themselves between this message's formatting fragments.
        let table = r["table"].as_str().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()).map(|t| format!(" {t}")).unwrap_or_default();
        // A lookup refusal whose row the read rule hid: name that layer, since the author's later `require` never ran.
        let hidden = r["hiddenBy"]["table"].as_str().map(|t| format!("; the lookup's {t} row is hidden by its read rule at {} (a hidden row reads as absent at a lookup, before any later require)", r["hiddenBy"]["readRuleSite"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"))).unwrap_or_default();
        let kind = if r["preflight"].as_bool() == Some(true) { "auth preflight probe" } else { "refusal" };
        let line = if self.detail { format!("dev {kind}: {}:{table} — {} at {}{hidden}; execution site {}; phase {}; generation {}; store {}; instance {}; code {}; count {}; window 1s; first request {}; first job {}; first time {}; first response {}; `snapback4 why recent --instance {}`\n",
            r["operation"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["clause"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["site"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"), r["executionSite"], r["phase"], r["generation"], identity["storeId"], identity["instance"], r["code"], entry.count, r["requestId"], r["jobId"], r["at"], r["responseSequence"], identity["instance"].as_str().unwrap_or("")) }
        else {
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.5 — wave 6 #19: the human signal first, one short line; the record stays in `why recent`.
            let on = r["table"].as_str().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()).map(|t| format!(" on {t}")).unwrap_or_default();
            let hidden = r["hiddenBy"]["table"].as_str().map(|t| format!("; the {t} row is hidden by its read rule at {} (hidden reads as absent)", r["hiddenBy"]["readRuleSite"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"))).unwrap_or_default();
            let times = if entry.count > 1 { format!(" ×{}", entry.count) } else { String::new() };
            format!("dev {kind}: {} {} at {} ({}{on}){hidden}{times} — snapback4 why recent\n", r["operation"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["code"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["site"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"), r["clause"].as_str().unwrap_or(""))
        };
        let _ = out.write_all(line.as_bytes());
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;
    #[test]
    fn shutdown_diagnostic_cannot_split_a_counted_refusal_line() {
        // Lifecycle diagnostics bypass Rust's stderr mutex. Simulate one
        // arriving after the writer's first fragment, as observed at shutdown.
        struct Interrupted { bytes: Vec<u8>, inserted: bool }
        impl std::io::Write for Interrupted {
            fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {
                self.bytes.extend_from_slice(bytes);
                if !self.inserted {
                    self.inserted = true;
                    self.bytes.extend_from_slice(b"SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT verb=dev generation=1 thread=\"worker\" reason=\"shutdown\"\n");
                }
                Ok(bytes.len())
            }
            fn flush(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<()> { Ok(()) }
        }
        let mut out = Interrupted { bytes: Vec::new(), inserted: false };
        let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
        let now = Instant::now();
        let identity = json!({"storeId":"store", "instance":"instance"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"add", "code":"E_RULE", "table":"rows",
            "clause":"insert rule", "phase":"insert rule", "site":"schema.q:5:3",
            "executionSite":"add:3", "generation":1, "requestId":2});
        for _ in 0..8 { console.push_at(record.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
        assert!(out.bytes.is_empty(), "the aggregation window is still open");
        console.flush_all(&identity, &mut out);
        let text = String::from_utf8(out.bytes).unwrap();
        let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 2, "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].starts_with("dev refusal: add: rows — insert rule at schema.q:5:3;"), "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].contains("code \"E_RULE\"; count 8; window 1s; first request 2;"), "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].ends_with("`snapback4 why recent --instance instance`"), "{text}");
        assert_eq!(lines[1], "SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT verb=dev generation=1 thread=\"worker\" reason=\"shutdown\"", "{text}");
    }

        struct Interleaved(Vec<u8>);
        impl Write for Interleaved {
            fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {

            let refuse = |diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>, message: String, rewrite: String| diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", message, rewrite).guide("writes"));

                continue;

//! Referential actions: what deleting a referenced row does to the rows that
//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.
use super::*;
pub use reservations::Price;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum OnDelete {
    Restrict,
    Cascade,
    SetNull,
    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    /// their target.
    Keep,
}

impl OnDelete {
    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    }
    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
}

/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    pub child: &'a str,
    pub table: &'a Table,
    pub column: &'a str,
    pub action: OnDelete,
}

/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }

/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;

impl Schema {
    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }

    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }

    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
        }
        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
            let columns = index.columns();
            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    }

    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        let declared = table.constraints.iter().find_map(|c| match c {
            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.len() == 1 && by[0] == column => Some(*max),
            _ => None,

        let unique = table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns() == [column]).then_some(1);
        declared.into_iter().chain(unique).min()
    }

    pub fn cap_skips_null(&self, table: &Table, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> bool {
        by.iter().zip(group).any(|(column, value)| *value == Value::Null && table.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

The durable mutation path and actual ephemeral HTTP route use the same seat formatter. The developer hidden counter is kept outside serialized responses and receipts, and the persona checks are gated to development. I’m checking the final edge cases in lint proofs and key selection before returning the verdict.

    fn check_constraints(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Option<&Value>, new: &Value, effect: Effect) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).expect("validated");
        let id = new.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.1 — an action's own write consults no rule: probing the child's read rule would draw the shared allowance only where a child exists.
        let readable = match old { Some(old) if effect == Effect::Update && !self.engine_action => {
                !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, old)?
                    && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, old)?
            }
            _ => true };
        if effect != Effect::Delete {
            for (name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, index)| index.unique) {
                // byId can only return this id (already checked on insert).
                // An unchanged admitted unique key cannot acquire a collision.
                if name == "byId" { continue; }
                let key = index_values(index, new);
                if old.is_some_and(|old| index_values(index, old) == key) { continue; }
                if key.iter().any(|value| *value == Value::Null) {
                    continue;
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — holding the target does not cover a different unique population.
                if self.device && effect == Effect::Update && !self.device_work(|runner| {
                        runner.upsert_absence_proven(table_name, name, &key)
                    })? {
                    return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, name, &key));
                }
                if let Some(existing) = self.collision_lookup(table_name, name, index, &key, Some(&id), effect)
                    .map_err(|error| {
                        if !readable && matches!(error, RuntimeError::Rule { .. }) { self.no_write_target() } else { error
                        }
                    })?
                    && existing.field("id")?.as_str()? != id
                {
                    self.observe_sequence_columns(table_name, new, &index.columns(), effect == Effect::Insert)?;
                    if effect == Effect::Update && !readable {
                        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                        self.refusing_clause(table_name, &format!("unique {}", index.columns().join(", ")));
                        return Err(self.no_write_target());
                    }
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.9 — the dev log names the `unique` line that refused (wave 3 I3).
                    self.refusing_clause(table_name, &format!("unique {}", index.columns().join(", ")));
                    let declared=index.components.iter().map(|part| match part {
                        crate::schema::IndexComponent::Calendar(k) if k.function=="bucket" => format!("bucket({}, {}{})",k.column,k.millis,if k.offset==0 {String::new()} else {format!(", {}",k.offset)}),
                        crate::schema::IndexComponent::Calendar(k)=>format!("{}({}{})",k.function,k.column,if k.offset==0 {String::new()} else {format!(", {}",k.offset)}),
                        _=>part.key_name().cloned().unwrap_or_else(||"<key>".into()),
                    }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    let detail = format!("{table_name}: unique {declared} already has a row where {declared} = {}", key.iter().map(describe).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
                    if let Some(refusal) = self.schema.unique_refusals.get(table_name).and_then(|indexes| indexes.get(name)) {
                        return Err(RuntimeError::NamedConstraint { code: refusal.code.clone(), message: refusal.message.clone(), detail });
                    }
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — the public code names uniqueness;
                    // diagnostics use declared columns, never planner index names.
                    return Err(RuntimeError::NamedConstraint { code: "E_UNIQUE".into(), message: detail.clone(), detail });
                }
            }
        }
        for constraint in &table.constraints {
            match constraint {
                Constraint::Capabilities(_) | Constraint::Recorded | Constraint::Unique { .. } | Constraint::Text { .. } | Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert { .. } => {}
                Constraint::AppendOnly => {
                    if effect != Effect::Insert {
                        return Err(RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("{table_name} is append-only")));
//! A positional unique key followed by named tests is named-key sugar.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — only declared unique keys resolve positions; extra equalities test one row.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent_walk as walk};
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site}, print};
use std::{borrow::Cow, collections::BTreeMap};

pub(super) fn keys<'a>(table: &TableInfo, parts: &'a [KeyPart], index: Option<&str>, site: &Site, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, [KeyPart]>> {
    if !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) || !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) { return Ok(Cow::Borrowed(parts)); }
    let prefix = parts.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
    if let Some(late) = parts[prefix..].iter().find(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        let ordered = parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).chain(parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_some())).map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&late.site, "E_QUARRY_KEY_ORDER", "positional key components come before named column tests",
            format!("{}[{ordered}]", table.decl.name)).guide("reads"));
    }
    let declared: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|key| key.unique
        && !synthesized.contains(&(table.decl.name.clone(), key.name.clone()))).collect();
    let candidates: Vec<_> = if let Some(types) = types {
        let read = ReadExpr { table: table.decl.name.clone(), keys: parts[..prefix].to_vec(), index: index.map(str::to_owned), site: site.clone(),
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() };
        super::positional_keys::candidates(table, &read, types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|key| key.unique && key.columns.len() == prefix).collect()
    } else {
        // Before types are known, do not silently turn `roles[viewer,
        // role = 'staff']` into an id lookup merely because byId has the
        // positional prefix's arity. A declared composite key covered by the
        // positional prefix plus named suffix must reach the typed pass, which
        // prints the fully named repair.
        let named: Vec<&str> = parts[prefix..].iter().filter_map(|part| part.column.as_deref()).collect();
        let partial_composite = declared.iter().any(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix && key.columns.len() <= parts.len()
            && key.columns[prefix..].iter().all(|column| named.contains(&column.as_str()))
            && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name));
        if partial_composite { Vec::new() }
        else { declared.iter().copied().filter(|key| key.columns.len() == prefix && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name)).collect() }
    };
    let chosen = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [chosen] if table.total_unique(chosen) => *chosen,
        _ => {
            let suggested = candidates.first().copied().or_else(|| declared.iter().copied().find(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix)).or_else(|| declared.first().copied());
            let descriptions = declared.iter().map(|key| format!("({})", key.columns.join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
            let rewrite = if let Some(key) = suggested {
                let mut named = key.columns.iter().enumerate().map(|(at, column)| {
                    let value = parts.get(at).filter(|_| at < prefix).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value))
                        .or_else(|| parts.iter().find(|part| part.column.as_ref() == Some(column)).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value)))
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("<{column}>"));
                    format!("{column} = {value}")
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                named.extend(parts[prefix..].iter().filter(|part| !key.columns.contains(part.column.as_ref().unwrap())).map(spelling));
                format!("{}[{}]", table.decl.name, named.join(", "))
            } else { format!("{}[id = <id>, {}]", table.decl.name, parts[prefix..].iter().map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
                format!("{} needs one full declared unique key with non-null columns before named tests; {prefix} positional components match {} keys; declared keys: {descriptions}", table.decl.name, candidates.len()), rewrite.clone())
                .source_rewrite(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey, rewrite).guide("reads"));
        }
    };
    let mut named = parts.to_vec();
    for (part, column) in named.iter_mut().zip(&chosen.columns) { part.column = Some(column.clone()); }
    Ok(Cow::Owned(named))
}

fn spelling(part: &KeyPart) -> String {
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {}", print::expression(&part.value)), None => print::expression(&part.value) }
}

pub(super) fn read<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    read_typed(table, read, catalog, None)
}

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,
        };
        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
    };
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules { walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut visit); }
        for (value, _) in &mut table.decl.final_exists { walk::expression(value, &mut visit); }
    }
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        walk::document(doc, &mut visit);
        for view in &mut doc.views { for branch in &mut view.branches {
            if let Some(predicate) = &mut branch.predicate { walk::expression(predicate, &mut visit); }
            walk::shape(&mut branch.fields, &mut visit);
        } }
    }
    catalog.shapes = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.shapes.iter().cloned().map(|shape| (shape.name.clone(), shape))).collect();
    catalog.functions = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.functions.iter().cloned().map(|function| (function.name.clone(), function))).collect();
}
    }
}

/// Named columns for a key: named parts as written; a single `id`- or
/// `t`-typed positional part is `id`; otherwise the one declared unique key
/// of the same arity whose column types accept the arguments in order.
fn resolve_key(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], kinds: &[Option<Kind>]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().all(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        let names: Vec<String> = keys.iter().map(|k| k.column.clone().unwrap()).collect();
        for n in &names {
            if n.contains('.') || table.col(n).is_none() {
                return no("key-column", format!("{}[{n}]", table.name));
            }
        }
        return Ok(names);
    }
    if keys.iter().any(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        return no("key-mixed", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    if kinds.iter().any(Option::is_none) {
        return no("key-untyped", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    let kinds: Vec<Kind> = kinds.iter().map(|k| k.clone().unwrap()).collect();
    if let [Kind::Id(sort)] = kinds.as_slice() {
        if matches!(sort, IdSort::Plain) || matches!(sort, IdSort::Ref(t) if *t == table.name) {
            return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
        }
    }
    let fits: Vec<&Vec<String>> = table
        .uniques
        .iter()
        .filter(|u| u.len() == kinds.len() && u.iter().zip(&kinds).all(|(c, k)| table.col(c).is_some_and(|ck| accepts(&ck, k))))
        .collect();
    match fits.as_slice() {
        [one] => Ok((*one).clone()),
        _ => no("key-unresolved", format!("{}[..] matches {} unique keys", table.name, fits.len())),
    }
}

/// The unique key selected by a rule probe. Named equalities may contain
/// residual predicates beyond the point key; the binder selects the shortest
/// covered total unique, then the first declared candidate. Positional probes
/// keep their declared-only, type-directed resolution above.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — nullable residual membership equalities are distinct from the selected point key.
fn resolve_probe_point(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], columns: &[String]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        return Ok(columns.to_vec());
    }
    if columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") {
        return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
    }
    let total = |unique: &[String]| {
        unique.iter().all(|column| {
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }


    /// Ordinary data reads keep their existing meter and cut semantics. This
    /// scope grants storage access without disabling or restoring any charge.
    /// Hidden authorization must use authorized_scan's prepaid reservation.
    pub(crate) fn metered_scan<T>(&mut self, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        scan(self, &Authorization::internal())
    }

    /// A rule whose logical allowance the caller has already charged, possibly
    /// to a cut, still evaluates its witnesses under the physical allowance;
    /// the caller decides what its cut means for the answer (LLP 3000.014 §5).
    pub(super) fn prepaid_witnesses<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || !self.context.is_system());
        let result = work(self, &Authorization::internal());
        self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — admission depends on declared bounds, never on
    // occupants. Private scratch cannot cut the caller's prepaid scan.
    pub(crate) fn authorized_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.prepaid_scan(allowance, true, scan)
    }

    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
/// one; a device treats every principal as connected and lets the server
/// decide (`Runner::presence_unknown`).
pub trait Presence: std::fmt::Debug + Send + Sync {
    /// How many connections `principal` holds open now.
    fn connections(&self, principal: &str) -> u64;
    /// A single observation: (live connections, grace deadline in engine time).
    fn effect_presence(&self, principal: &str) -> (u64, Option<i64>) { (self.connections(principal), None) }
    /// Pin an unconfirmed poll's deadline to the mutation's commit clock.
    fn effect_presence_at(&self, principal: &str, now: i64) -> (u64, Option<i64>) {
        let (live, deadline) = self.effect_presence(principal);
        (live, if live > 0 { Some(now.saturating_add(PRESENCE_CONFIRMATION.as_millis() as i64)) } else { deadline },
        )
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum LoadMode { Import, Restore }

/// Who runs the program, with what, and when.

pub struct Context {

    pub retained_search: bool,

    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub search_retained_order: Option<(Vec<i64>, bool)>,
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub(crate) search_retained_candidates: Vec<(String, i64, bool)>,
    pub read_spent: Option<crate::meter::Metered>,
    pub viewer: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub args: BTreeMap<String, Value>,
    pub now: Option<i64>,
    pub read_time: Option<i64>,
    pub(crate) row_provenance: bool,
    /// Digest only: the core resolves it in the admission transaction.
    pub(crate) session_digest: Option<String>,
    /// Original HTTP arguments, before typed decoding or default insertion.

//! A positional unique key followed by named tests is named-key sugar.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — only declared unique keys resolve positions; extra equalities test one row.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent_walk as walk};
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site}, print};
use std::{borrow::Cow, collections::BTreeMap};

pub(super) fn keys<'a>(table: &TableInfo, parts: &'a [KeyPart], index: Option<&str>, site: &Site, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, [KeyPart]>> {
    if !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) || !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) { return Ok(Cow::Borrowed(parts)); }
    let prefix = parts.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
    if let Some(late) = parts[prefix..].iter().find(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        let ordered = parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).chain(parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_some())).map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&late.site, "E_QUARRY_KEY_ORDER", "positional key components come before named column tests",
            format!("{}[{ordered}]", table.decl.name)).guide("reads"));
    }
    let declared: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|key| key.unique
        && !synthesized.contains(&(table.decl.name.clone(), key.name.clone()))).collect();
    let candidates: Vec<_> = if let Some(types) = types {
        let read = ReadExpr { table: table.decl.name.clone(), keys: parts[..prefix].to_vec(), index: index.map(str::to_owned), site: site.clone(),
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() };
        super::positional_keys::candidates(table, &read, types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|key| key.unique && key.columns.len() == prefix).collect()
    } else {
        // Before types are known, do not silently turn `roles[viewer,
        // role = 'staff']` into an id lookup merely because byId has the
        // positional prefix's arity. A declared composite key covered by the
        // positional prefix plus named suffix must reach the typed pass, which
        // prints the fully named repair.
        let named: Vec<&str> = parts[prefix..].iter().filter_map(|part| part.column.as_deref()).collect();
        let partial_composite = declared.iter().any(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix && key.columns.len() <= parts.len()
            && key.columns[prefix..].iter().all(|column| named.contains(&column.as_str()))
            && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name));
        if partial_composite { Vec::new() }
        else { declared.iter().copied().filter(|key| key.columns.len() == prefix && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name)).collect() }
    };
    let chosen = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [chosen] if table.total_unique(chosen) => *chosen,
        _ => {
            let suggested = candidates.first().copied().or_else(|| declared.iter().copied().find(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix)).or_else(|| declared.first().copied());
            let descriptions = declared.iter().map(|key| format!("({})", key.columns.join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
            let rewrite = if let Some(key) = suggested {
                let mut named = key.columns.iter().enumerate().map(|(at, column)| {
                    let value = parts.get(at).filter(|_| at < prefix).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value))
                        .or_else(|| parts.iter().find(|part| part.column.as_ref() == Some(column)).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value)))
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("<{column}>"));
                    format!("{column} = {value}")
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                named.extend(parts[prefix..].iter().filter(|part| !key.columns.contains(part.column.as_ref().unwrap())).map(spelling));
                format!("{}[{}]", table.decl.name, named.join(", "))
            } else { format!("{}[id = <id>, {}]", table.decl.name, parts[prefix..].iter().map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
                format!("{} needs one full declared unique key with non-null columns before named tests; {prefix} positional components match {} keys; declared keys: {descriptions}", table.decl.name, candidates.len()), rewrite.clone())
                .source_rewrite(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey, rewrite).guide("reads"));
        }
    };
    let mut named = parts.to_vec();
    for (part, column) in named.iter_mut().zip(&chosen.columns) { part.column = Some(column.clone()); }
    Ok(Cow::Owned(named))
}

fn spelling(part: &KeyPart) -> String {
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {}", print::expression(&part.value)), None => print::expression(&part.value) }
}

pub(super) fn read<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    read_typed(table, read, catalog, None)
}

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,
        };
        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
            format!("immutable {reference}"),
        ));
    }
    one(&parent, tables, done, visiting)?;
    let parent_info = &tables[&parent];
    let parent_read = parent_info
        .decl
        .rules
        .iter()
        .find(|r| r.effect == Effect::Read)
        .cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| RuleDecl {
            effect: Effect::Read,
            bindings: vec![],
            value: Expr::Name {
                name: if parent_info.decl.public.is_some() {
                    "allow"
                } else {
                    "deny"
                }
                .into(),
                site: site.clone(),
            },
            site: site.clone(),
        });
    let mut value = parent_read.value.clone();
    let mut fields = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut captures = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut errors = Vec::new();
    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |expr| {
        if let Expr::Member {
            base, name: field, ..
        } = expr
            && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. })
        {
            let immutable = field == "id"
                || parent_info.captures.contains_key(field)
                || parent_info
                    .decl
                    .immutable
                    .iter()
                    .any(|(fs, _)| fs.contains(field));
            if !immutable {
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.6 — lane 082: a reassignable audience column cannot be
                // captured; an online-only child can probe the live parent row instead.
                let probes = (info.decl.sync.is_none()).then(|| parent_probes(&parent_read.value, &parent, &reference)).flatten()
                    .map(|rule| format!("; or, when {field} must change (an online-only table reads it live), replace `read through .{reference}` with `read <- {rule}`"))
                    .unwrap_or_default();
                errors.push(fail(
                    format!("{parent}.{field} is captured by {name} and must be immutable. `read through` freezes this audience input when the child is inserted. If it must remain mutable, choose the intended history: copy it into a required immutable audience column on {name}, check `.copy = .{reference}.{field}` in {name}'s insert rule, and use an ordinary read rule over the copy to retain creation-time authority; or keep {name} online only and probe the live parent so existing children follow later changes"),
                    format!("immutable {field}  -- on table {parent}{probes}"),
                ));
            }
            let ty = if field == "id" {
                Some(ScalarType {
                    kind: TypeKind::Ref {
                        table: parent.clone(),
                    },
                    optional: false,
                })
            } else {
                if run.state == snapback4_core::JobState::Done { 0 } else { 1 }
            }
            Err(error) => {
                eprintln!("{op}: {error}");
                1
            }
        };
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#3 — the developer's own run collects the refusing clause, as dev does.
    let trace = snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace::default();
    let context = Context::new(principal, args).at(now).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
    if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query {
        match engine.query(op, context) {
            Ok(outcome) => {
                let body = if outcome.unavailable { json!({ "loading": true, "data": null, "complete": false, "metered": outcome.metered }) }
                    else { json!({ "data": wire::to_json(&outcome.data), "complete": outcome.complete, "cut": outcome.cut, "next": outcome.next, "metered": outcome.metered }) };
                println!("{}", if options.json { body.to_string() } else { serde_json::to_string_pretty(&body).unwrap() });
                0
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                println!("{}", if options.json { json!({ "denied": refusal, "complete": false }).to_string() } else { format!("denied {refusal}") });
                run_refusal::print(run_refusal::local(&trace, &bound.refusal_sites), Some(source.files()));
                1
            }
        }
    } else {
        match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
            Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                WriteState::Sent { seq, result } => {
                    let body = json!({ "state": "sent", "seq": seq, "result": wire::to_json(&result), "events": outcome.events, "metered": outcome.metered });
                    println!("{}", if options.json { body.to_string() } else { serde_json::to_string_pretty(&body).unwrap() });
                    0
                }
                WriteState::Failed { why, current } => {
                    let body = json!({ "state": "failed", "why": why, "current": current.map(|c| wire::to_json(&c)) });
                    println!("{}", if options.json { body.to_string() } else { format!("failed {why}") });
                    run_refusal::print(run_refusal::local(&trace, &bound.refusal_sites), Some(source.files()));
                    1
                }
            },
            Err(error) => {
                eprintln!("{error}");
                2
            }
        }
    }
}
        self.tx.authorizing.set(state.authorizing);
        self.meter.restore(state.mark);
        self.meter.ceilings = state.ceilings;
        self.meter.cut = state.cut;
        if !state.nested { self.caller_heap_ceiling = None; }
        if let Some(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::HeapBytes, site, .. }) = error {
            self.meter.capped_at.get_or_insert((MeterKind::HeapBytes, site.clone()));
        }
    }

}

    pub fn asset_declaration(&mut self,owner:&str,id:&str)->Result<Option<serde_json::Value>,RuntimeError>{
        use rusqlite::OptionalExtension;
        let tx=self.store.transaction()?;
        let binding:Option<(Option<String>,String)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT placement,state FROM _asset_intents WHERE owner=?1 AND intent=?2",[owner,id],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?))).optional()?;
        if binding.as_ref().is_some_and(|(_,s)|s=="cancelled"){return Err(RuntimeError::Asset{code:"E_UPLOAD_CANCELLED",message:"this intent was cancelled".into()});}
        let result=binding.and_then(|(p,_)|p).map(|p|crate::json_codec::from_text(&p).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))).transpose()?;
        tx.rollback()?;Ok(result)
    }

    /// A device-owned placing id cannot become an ephemeral execution after adoption.
    // @ref LLP 3000.038.000#3 — media placement always settles through a durable receipt.
    pub fn require_non_media_ephemeral(&self,owner:&str,id:Option<&str>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
        if let Some(id)=id
            && self.store.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _asset_intents WHERE owner=?1 AND intent=?2)",[owner,id],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Asset{code:"E_UPLOAD_INTENT",message:"upload-and-place requires a durable mutation; this operation became ephemeral".into()});
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Replay before current-schema admission; dedup receipts outlive collection.
    pub fn asset_intent(&mut self, owner: &str, id: &str, fingerprint: &str) -> Result<Option<Asset>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let result = assets::replay_intent(&tx, owner, id, fingerprint)?;
    fn collision_lookup(&mut self, table: &str, name: &str, index: &crate::schema::Index, key: &[Value], except: Option<&str>, effect: Effect) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let probes = super::rules::rule_probes(&self.schema.tables[table].rules.read);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {
            let found = if runner.schema.tables[table].not_before.is_some() {
                runner.lookup_write_occupant(table, name, index, key)?
            } else {
                runner.live_lookup(allowance, table, name, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
            };
            if let Some(row) = &found && Some(row.field("id")?.as_str()?) != except {
                runner.observe_sequence_columns(table, row, &index.columns(), effect == Effect::Insert)?;
                runner.visible_collision(table, row, effect, &format!("unique {}", index.columns().join(", ")))?;
            }
            Ok(found)
        })
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — hidden collisions use the ordinary write-rule refusal
    fn visible_collision(
        &mut self,
        table: &str,
        row: &Value,
        effect: Effect,
        clause: &str,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let mut image = row.clone();
        let definition = self.schema.table(table).expect("validated");
        materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, definition);
        if self.inactive_write_target(table, &image)? || !self.check_rule(table, Effect::Read, None, &image)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table, effect, clause));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// `after insert <- exists t[...]` holds for every row inserted this
    /// transaction; checked once the program has run.
    pub(super) fn check_final_exists(&mut self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let inserted = std::mem::take(&mut self.inserted);
        for (table_name, row) in &inserted {
            let table = self.schema.table(table_name).expect("validated");
            for constraint in &table.constraints {
                let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert { table: target, index, key } = constraint else { continue;
                };
                let refusal = || {
                    RuntimeError::Constraint(format!("inserting into {table_name} requires a matching row in {target} by the end of the mutation"))
                };
                let has_references = key.iter().any(|term| term.reference_source().is_some());
    }

    /// Whether a value of `self` may be stored where `expected` is declared.
    pub fn assignable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        if self == expected {
            return true;
        }
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Null, Ty::Optional(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Optional(inner), Ty::Optional(target)) => inner.assignable_to(target),
            (value, Ty::Optional(target)) => value.assignable_to(target),
            (Ty::Text(a), Ty::Text(b)) => a <= b,
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20 — enum widening is static and total: every variant fits the text bound and character domain.
            (Ty::Enum(variants), Ty::Text(max)) => variants.iter().all(|v| v.chars().count() <= *max as usize && snapback4_core::engine::validate::disallowed_text_control(v).is_none()),
            // A text literal in an enum position is a variant; the binder
            // checks membership where it knows the literal.
            (Ty::Text(_), Ty::Enum(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Id, Ty::Ref(_)) | (Ty::Ref(_), Ty::Id) | (Ty::Id, Ty::Principal) | (Ty::Principal, Ty::Id) => true,
            (Ty::Ref(a), Ty::Ref(b)) => a == b,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Time) | (Ty::Time, Ty::Int) => true,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Decimal(_)) | (Ty::Int, Ty::Money(..)) => true,
            (a, b) if a.sequence().is_some() && b.sequence().is_some() => {
                let (a, am) = a.sequence().unwrap();
                let (b, bm) = b.sequence().unwrap();
                a.assignable_to(&b) && am <= bm
            },
            (Ty::Object(a) | Ty::RowShape(a) | Ty::RowObject(a), Ty::Object(b) | Ty::RowShape(b) | Ty::RowObject(b)) => a.len() == b.len() && a.iter().all(|(name, ty)| b.get(name).is_some_and(|target| ty.assignable_to(target))),
            (Ty::Enum(a), Ty::Enum(b)) => a.iter().all(|v| b.contains(v)),
            (Ty::Unknown, _) | (_, Ty::Unknown) | (_, Ty::Json) => true,
            _ => false,
        }
    }

    /// A heap-bound allocation can be written; the row validator checks its
    /// actual length. It cannot claim a smaller bound inside an inline helper.
    pub(crate) fn writable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Optional(a), Ty::Optional(b)) => a.writable_to(b),
            (a, Ty::Optional(b)) => a.writable_to(b) || *a == Ty::Null,
            (Ty::Text(u32::MAX), Ty::Text(_)) => true,
            (Ty::List(a, am), Ty::List(b, bm)) => a.writable_to(b) && (*am == u32::MAX || am <= bm),
            _ => self.assignable_to(expected),
        }
    }

    /// Whether two values may meet in `=`, `!=` or a key position.
    pub fn comparable(&self, other: &Ty) -> bool {
        let a = self.required();
        let b = other.required();
        a == b || a.assignable_to(&b) || b.assignable_to(&a) || a == Ty::Null || b == Ty::Null
    }

    /// The type both branches can share. Row-kind conflicts are errors at
    /// their first join, including inside objects and collections.
    pub fn join(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, false)
    }

    /// Nonempty list items must never erase a conflict into Unknown: a later
    /// item could otherwise recover a type the earlier values do not have.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — literals and concatenation share sound item joins.
    pub(crate) fn join_item(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, true)
    }

    fn join_inner(&self, other: &Ty, strict: bool) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        if self == other { return Ok(self.clone()); }
        // Absence adds nullability; it supplies no competing wildcard kind.
        if *self == Ty::Null { return Ok(other.clone().optional()); }
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — declaration-only scratch is admitted before any
    // candidate/witness access. The logical heap retains only disclosed output.
    pub(super) fn authorized_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, true, work)
    }

    /// Count certification follows an admitted member and source-group verdict.
    /// Nested authorization still shares any enclosing physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn disclosed_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, false, work)
    }

    pub(super) fn reserved_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }

    /// The fixed charge a delete of a `parent` row reserves before it probes,
    /// cascades or clears any row, over every meter: the closure's rows and
    /// their identities, every restrict probe (one seek per closure row, plus
    /// the closure rows it skips), every set-null row, and one engine write of
    /// each, priced from the source-write plan the engine already exports. A
    /// function of the schema only — caps, the reference graph, each table's
    /// indexes and row size, and the exported plans — so a meter, an `E_BOUND`
    /// abort or the physical allowance never depends on how many (hidden) rows
    /// exist, and every host charges alike: a device's projection keeps every
    /// table that can carry an action and every exported price. `None` when the
    /// closure is unbounded (a cycle or a missing cap) or a written table has no
    /// fixed write price. Heap is the closure's identity scratch plus the largest
    /// single row's transient work, plus event images retained until commit.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — the reservation is schema-fixed; LLP 3000.015 §2's law for work shaped by hidden rows.
    pub fn delete_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let bounds = self.closure_bounds(parent)?;
        let mut price = [0_u64; 7];
        let mut heap = 0_u64;
        let mut identities = 0_u64;
        let mut gathered = 0_u64;
        let rows_of = |name: &str| bounds.iter().find(|(t, _)| t == name).map_or(0, |(_, n)| *n);
        for (table, n) in &bounds {
            let n = *n;
            if table != parent {
                // Fetched by the closure walk and again, current, before its delete.
                let unit = self.unit_write(table)?;
                heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n)));
                identities = identities.saturating_add(n);
            }
            for r in self.referencing(table) {
                // A capped walk holds one page of child images per seek.
                let row = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(r.table);
                heap = heap.max(row.saturating_mul(REFERENCE_PAGE as u64));
                let seeks = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && r.table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|t| t.reference != r.column) { 2 } else { 1 };
                // A walk over a capped group: a seek per page, and a last, short page per range.
                let walk = |cap: u64| seeks * n + cap.saturating_mul(n) / REFERENCE_PAGE as u64;
                // Every reached row runs the lifetime hook for every through
                // child, including children the closure already cascaded away.
                let through = r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column);
                if through && table != parent { add(&mut price, [3_u64, 0, 3, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n))); }
                match r.action {
                    // A read-through child's probe: three seeks per closure row (LLP 3000.014 §6).
                    OnDelete::Restrict if through => {},
                    // One probe per closure row, a row per seek: it may skip the closure
                    // rows that name it (each names one target per reference), then stops
                    // at its first outside witness.
                    OnDelete::Restrict => {
                        let probe = seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n;
                        add(&mut price, [2 * probe, probe, seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::Cascade => {
